## 0 · The year in one paragraph

Valentin is a **bartender** who works for several unrelated hospitality employers and, separately,
takes **contractor work paid on Form 1099-NEC**. He files **Single**, has **no dependants**, lives in
**Florida** (so no state return has ever been required), and buys his health insurance on the
**Marketplace**, which puts **Form 8962** on his return every year. 2025 differs from 2024 in three
ways that matter, and **each one moves money**: his **contractor income went up by an order of
magnitude** while his W-2 wages went down; **2025 is the first year of the tips deduction**, and he
is a tipped worker — ⚠️ **his occupation on the return is Bartender, which IS listed, but whether the
role at the largest employer was is NOT established (§6 item ④)**; and his Schedule C, which ran at a **loss** in 2024 —
producing a **qualified-business-loss carryforward into 2025** — runs at a **profit** in 2025.
✅ **Established as prepared (§9B), at 19,531 — but only because the expense side is EMPTY** *(§9F)*,
so the figure is a ceiling, not a result. It turns on **self-employment tax** and, for the first time,
makes the **self-employed health insurance deduction** available — ⛔ **which was left blank
(§9H item 3)** — and that deduction is **circular with the premium tax credit**.
**Everything hanging off the profit still moves if the expenses arrive.**

🛑 **The return WAS PREPARED on 2026-09-13 — ⛔ and NOT FILED; it has not been transmitted.**
🔑 **One sentence this paragraph carried on 2026-09-12 is now a description of what was prepared:**
*"a Schedule C with income and no expenses is not a return, it is a mistake waiting to be filed."* ⚠️ **It was prepared that way as a DECLARED, DOCUMENTED decision**
*(§9F; Double note 524890)*, because the client never returned the P&L template. ⛔ **That makes it a
decision to confirm or overturn, not a defect to flag** — but its cost compounds four ways and §9F
states it.
🔑 **The root cause has not moved:** he answered the organizer's income-source question with
`Wages (W2)` **only**, which is conditional logic, so the organizer never asked him a single business
question. ⓘ *Of the other three blockers, two closed on 2026-09-13 — the VoiceCapital 1099 (§3B) and
the Form 1095-A (§9A); the tips question is now §9D.*

## 2 · Adjusting entries made before preparing

**None, and none is possible.** There are no books. Double records `platform: none` and
`Bookkeeping: N/A` — this client has no ledger, no QuickBooks file and no P&L. Every figure on the
return comes from a third-party document or from the client's own statement.

🔑 **That is the structural reason the Schedule C expense side is empty**: there is nothing to derive
it from. It has to be asked for.

>
> 🔴 ⚠️ **HOW IT WAS READ, AND IT IS NOT THE REDACTOR.** The document was read through **Google
> Drive's own indexed text**, exactly like the Compass Group W-2 in §3A. ⛔ **That route has no
> masking of any kind**, so it is the **second DOCUMENT** on this one client to arrive that way and
> the **third use of the route** — the search that surfaced the lead on 2026-09-12 was the same
> channel *(FOLLOW-UPS row 103; **§4 decision 6** records it)*. 🔑 **It also sets
> the ceiling on what the ties below can be**, and it is why they are now ranked instead of counted.

>
> **What the document says:** recipient **Valentin Volzhanskiy** at his **Fort Lauderdale** address;
> payer **VOICECAPITAL INC** (Aventura FL); **box 1 = 9,084.50**; calendar year **2025**; **no
> federal withholding**; the copy set as tax1099 produced it, **the payer's own Copy C included**.
> ⓘ *A 1099-NEC set runs to five copies; **how many of the five the file carries was not counted**,
> so the copies are context, not evidence.*
>
> ✅ **FOUR FIELDS CROSS-CHECK — but they are FOUR FIELDS OF ONE RECORD, not four observations, and
> this is the correction the review forced.** All four were keyed by the firm into tax1099 on
> **2026-01-31**; what makes any of them evidence is the **outside source each is checked against**,
> and those are not equal:
>
> | Field | Checked against | Weight |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Recipient TIN — last four** | 🔑 **The Compass Group W-2** *(§3A row 1)* — his employer's data, nothing the firm keyed | 🟢 **This is the tie that carries it — and it is narrower than "his W-2s".** ⛔ **The redactor emits `[SSN-n]` TAGS, never digits** *(`redact.py`)*, so W-2s 2-4, which were read through it, **cannot** have supplied a last four. **Only row 1 could** — and row 1 came off the **same unmasked Drive channel** as this 1099. 🔑 **So both sides of the strongest tie are OCR'd digits on one route**, and *"a mis-read digit is silent"* *(FOLLOW-UPS row 103)* cuts **both ways**. ⓘ *Inferred from the tooling and §3A, not from a record of which W-2 was compared.* |
> | **Fort Lauderdale address** | The **NEW** address on his 2025 organizer, not the old Hialeah one | 🟡 **Corroborative, with one open question.** The 1099 was created **2026-01-31** and the organizer completed **2026-04-15**, so the form **cannot** have been populated from the organizer — ⛔ **but whether the firm keyed it from its own client record is NOT established, and if it did, the tie is circular** |
> | **Account number** *(the 1099's payer-assigned box, not a bank account)* | The **Pro Title Agency** 1099 issued to him | 🟠 **Weakest.** Both forms came out of the same software against the same recipient record, so an identical value shows they share that record — useful, **not independent** |
> | **Recipient name in full** | — | 🟠 **The payer supplied it.** It is what makes the others worth checking, not a check itself |
>
> 🛑 **A CORRECTION TO THIS PAPER'S OWN EARLIER WORDING, kept because it is the lesson.** The first
> pass wrote *"a 1099-NEC **naming Valentin as recipient**"* and then, correctly, labelled it a lead.
> ⛔ **Those two do not sit together:** at that moment the Drive snippet had shown the **payer** block
> and the **file name** only — the recipient block was not legible — so the phrase asserted more than
> the evidence carried, even while the label hedged it. 🔑 **The hedge was right and the sentence was
> ahead of it.** ⚠️ **And be exact about what WAS legible, or the lesson softens itself:** the file
> **name** carried his name too — `ValentinVolzhanskiy_1728_12.pdf` — which is suggestive and is not
> a recipient block, and treating it as one is precisely the step that was taken. ✅ **Reading the
> document happened to confirm the earlier guess; it could as easily have contradicted it.**
> ⛔ **Note it did not settle "in the client's favour" — confirming 9,084.50 of unreported contractor
> income RAISES his self-employment tax, his AGI and his APTC repayment together.**
>
> ⚠️ **What is established is that the FIRM PREPARED it.** ⛔ **Not "issued" — issued means furnished
> to the recipient, and that is not established.** Whether it was transmitted to the IRS and
> furnished to him is a smaller, separate question; the file is the software's own output, which is
> strong evidence of preparation and is not a transmittal receipt.
> ⓘ **Also worth noting for context, not as a finding:** VoiceCapital and Pro Title Agency print the
> **same telephone number** on their 1099s, which is consistent with shared administration.

> 2026-09-12, and independently re-verified.** The 2025 Form 1040 **renumbered** — AGI is **line
> 11a**, not line 11.

> 🔑 **THE MULTI-EMPLOYER WORKSHEET IS THE WHOLE ANSWER, AND IT IS NOT ON THE FORM.**
> There is **no standalone Schedule 1-A instruction booklet** (`i1040s1a.pdf` returns 404). The
> instructions live inside the **2025 Instructions for Form 1040**, and the
> **"Qualified Tips From More Than One Employer Worksheet — Keep for Your Records"** is on
> **page 104**. Verbatim structure: for **each employer**, column (b) = qualified tips reported by
> that employer on Form W-2 **or** reported by the employee to that employer on Forms 4070;
> column (c) = qualified tips on Form 4137 column 1(c) for that same employer; column (d) = **the
> greater of (b) or (c)**. Then *"Add lines 1A through 1E, column (d), and enter this amount on
> Schedule 1-A, line 4c."*
> 🔑 **The comparison is made per employer, not on the combined totals.** That is the point of it.
> ⚠️ **Unsettled in the IRS text, not by us:** the worksheet has exactly five rows and, unlike the
> line-5 worksheet, carries **no** *"complete as many copies as needed"* instruction. He has four
> employers, so it does not bite here.
> ⚠️ **Also unsettled:** the **form** says enter `-0-` on lines 4a **and** 4b; the **instructions'**
> Line 4b paragraph only redirects to the worksheet without repeating `-0-`. Both are reported;
> neither is harmonised by us.

> 🟢 **BOX 14 COUNTS — THIS IS THE FINDING THAT PAYS FOR THE WHOLE REVIEW.**
> **Notice 2025-69 §III.A.1**, the Treasury/IRS administrative determination for tax year 2025,
> option **3**, verbatim: *"If an employer voluntarily chooses to report the amount of an employee's
> cash tips in **box 14 of Form W-2** (or on a separate statement), the employee **may use this
> amount** in determining the amount of qualified tips for tax year 2025."*
> Restated at **2025 Instructions for Form 1040, page 103**, method 3.
> 🔑 **The four methods are ALTERNATIVES, not a hierarchy** — *"you can figure your qualified tips
> using one of the methods described in paragraphs 1 through 4"* — and **option 3 is NOT conditioned
> on box 7 being empty.**
> ⚠️ **Its condition is that the EMPLOYER chose to report it.** The employee may not self-compute a
> box-14 figure. Employer 1's W-2 carries `TIPS 16,647.75` in box 14, which satisfies **that** condition.
>
> ### 🔴 BUT THE REPORTING ROUTE IS NOT ELIGIBILITY — AND TWO SCREENS BITE BEFORE IT
>
> 🛑 **Notice 2025-69 settles WHERE the figure may be read from. It does not make the amount a
> qualified tip.** ⛔ **An earlier pass of this paper treated "the employer reported it in box 14" as
> the whole test. It is not.** ⓘ *Strictly there are THREE screens; the third is a taxpayer-level gate
> that does not bite here and is recorded so nobody meets it cold on another client:* **a valid SSN,
> issued for employment before the return's due date including extensions** *(2025 Instructions for
> Form 1040 p. 104; Schedule 1-A Part II's own caution repeats it)*. ⚠️ **On a foreign-born client
> base that is not a formality** — ✅ **here it is satisfied: all four W-2s carry social security
> wages.**
> **Two statutory screens then run on the money**, and on **these** employers the first one is live:
>
> **① PAID VOLUNTARILY — service charges and automatic gratuities are OUT.** Verbatim, 2025
> Instructions for Form 1040 **p. 102**, heading *"Paid voluntarily, not subject to negotiation, and
> determined by the customer/payor"*:
> *"Amounts are qualified tips only if they are paid voluntarily and without any consequence in the
> event of nonpayment. **Qualified tips do not include service charges, automatic gratuities, or any
> other mandatory amounts automatically added to a customer's bill** by the vendor or the
> establishment, unless the customer is expressly provided an option to disregard or modify such
> charges, gratuities, and amounts without consequence."*
> 🔑 **The IRS's own Example 1 is this exact fact pattern:** a restaurant's automatic 18% charge on a
> party of six, printed on the tip line — *"Even though the restaurant distributed the amount to you
> and bussers"*, it is **not** a qualified tip. **Example 2 is the contrast**: a POS device offering
> 15/18/20/other/**no tip**, where the customer chose — that one qualifies.
> 🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS HERE AND NOT ON A TYPICAL BARTENDER:** **all FOUR of his 2025 employers are
> contract catering, events or concessions businesses** — *Compass Group*, *Creative Edge
> **Parties***, *DO & CO **Events***, *Professional **Concessions*** — which is precisely the sector
> where a **fixed service charge distributed to staff** is the norm rather than the exception.
> ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-09-13 — the earlier wording said "all three tip-bearing employers" and named
> the wrong three.** Per §3A only **TWO** employers report tips at all: **Compass Group** *(box 14)*
> and **Professional Concessions** *(box 7)*; **Creative Edge and DO & CO report none in either
> box.** 🔑 **The sector point survives intact and is the reason this screen exists** — it was the
> count and the names that were wrong. ⚠️ **And the W-2 itself is ambiguous in the same
> direction:** on employer 1, **box 7 is EMPTY while box 3 = box 1 = box 5**. An empty box 7 beside a
> box-14 line labelled `TIPS` is **at least as consistent with "these were never §6053-reported cash
> tips"** as with "the employer chose box 14 instead". ⛔ **The paper must not pick the favourable
> reading.**
>
> **② THE EMPLOYER'S TRADE OR BUSINESS MUST NOT BE AN SSTB.** Verbatim, same page: *"If your employer
> is in a specified service trade or business (SSTB), tips received as an employee of that employer
> are not qualified tips."* ⓘ **Catering and food service is not an SSTB on its face**, so this is a
> box to tick rather than a live risk — but it is a screen, and it is recorded so nobody re-derives it.
>
> ✅ **So the position splits in two, and only the first half is established:** the **reporting route**
> (box 14 may be used) is settled; **eligibility** is not, and it turns on a fact only the employer
> holds. 🛠️ **The question is §6 item ④.**
> ⛔ **Authority correction, caught in verification:** this is **not** OBBBA §70201(k). That
> subsection reaches only §§6041(a), 6041(d)(3), 6041A(a), 6041A(e)(3), 6050W(a) and 6050W(f)(2) —
> the **1099/payor** side — and **does not list §6051(a)(18)**, the Form W-2 provision. The
> permission is the administrative determination in Notice 2025-69 §III.A.1.

> ✅ **BARTENDER IS A LISTED OCCUPATION** — category *"Beverage and food service"*, Treasury Tipped
> Occupation Code **101**, *"Bartenders"*. His 2024 return states his occupation as **Bartender** on
> the signature line, and the organizer repeats it.
> ⚠️ **The occupation filter runs first:** only tips received *in* a listed occupation are qualified.
> If any of the four jobs was, say, a non-tipped kitchen or admin role, its tips are excluded.

**③ Form 8995 (2025)** _(Cat. No. 37806C, Created 9/12/25 — 17 lines, numbering unchanged from 2024)_

| Line | What | For this return |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The trade-or-business grid | the Schedule C |
| 2 | Total QBI or (loss) | |
| **3** | **"Qualified business net (loss) carryforward from the prior year"** | 🔴 **(2,005)** — the box is **preprinted with parentheses**; enter it **negative** |
| 4 | **Combine** lines 2 and 3; if zero or less, enter -0- | |
| 5 | line 4 × 20% | |
| 11 | Taxable income before QBI = **Form 1040 line 11a − line 12e − line 13b** | ⚠️ **the tips deduction reduces this base** |
| 15 | QBI deduction → **Form 1040 line 13a** | ⚠️ was line 13 in 2024 |
| 16 | Total qualified business (loss) carryforward → **next year's line 3** | |

> ⚠️ **AND ONE 2025 RULE THAT TIES THE TWO NEW THINGS TOGETHER — read at source, 2025 Instructions
> for Form 8995 and its QBI flow chart:** **tip income under §224 that is excluded from the net
> profit of the trade or business is NOT QBI.** 🔑 **It only bites if any of the 1099-NEC receipts
> turn out to be tips** *(Schedule 1-A line 5 — probably none here)*, **but if that answer is ever
> yes, the QBI figure moves as well as the tips figure.** Recorded so nobody re-derives it.

> ⚠️ **No IRS sentence says in words "enter line 3 as a negative."** The instructions were grepped
> and there is none. The conclusion rests on three things: the box is **preprinted with
> parentheses** (as are lines 7, 16 and 17 and nothing else on the form); line 4 says **"combine"**,
> which is algebraic; and the feeding figure — 2024 line 16 — is itself parenthesised. **Stated as
> an inference, because that is what it is.**
> 🔑 **2025 threshold for using Form 8995 rather than 8995-A: $197,300** (all filers other than MFJ).
> He is far below it.

> moves it — its 9,084.50 is *in* the gross (§3B). ⛔ **But "the expenses alone" would be wrong**, and
> three things still move the AGI: the **Schedule C expenses** nobody has asked for; the **§162(l)
> health-insurance deduction**, which is above the line — 🆕 ⚠️ **but §11A REVERSES this sentence on
> both counts: the deduction is NOT "computed off the premiums" (the APTC covered 100% of them, so
> the figure is the 1,625 repayment), and the band does NOT move on these figures.** ⛔ *Read §11A
> before using this paragraph;* and

### 3E · 🔴 THE VEHICLE — what 2024 did, why it is wrong, and what it costs in 2025

> 🔑 **Raised by Lilian, 2026-09-12:** *"en 2024 se subieron los gastos del teléfono y la gasolina…
> sin embargo, no se llenó la línea 9… ¿es esto un procedimiento correcto?"* **She is right, and the
> answer has three parts that are different in kind.** Everything below is quoted from the
> **2024** documents — the year in question — and re-checked against 2025.

#### ① Fuel for a business car belongs on LINE 9. Three separate texts say so.

| # | Source | Verbatim |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **2024 Instructions, Line 9** *(page C-7)* | *"If you deduct actual expenses: • **Include on line 9 the business portion of expenses for gasoline**, oil, repairs, insurance, license plates, etc.; and • **Show depreciation on line 13 and rent or lease payments on line 20a**."* 🔑 **Both bullets matter — line 9 takes the OPERATING costs only** |
| 2 | **2024 Instructions, Part V line 48** | *"Include all ordinary and necessary business expenses **not deducted elsewhere on Schedule C**."* |
| 3 | 🔑 **The 2024 FORM's own Part V caption** | *"List below business expenses **not included on lines 8–26, line 27b, or line 30**."* |

🔑 **The third is the strongest, and it is on the face of the form the client signs.** Line 9 sits
inside *"lines 8–26"*, so a car-fuel cost is **excluded from Part V by the form's own words.**
⚠️ *(2025 says the same with the renumbering: "not included on **lines 8-27a**, or line 30".)*

#### ② But the line is the SMALL half. What it changes, and what it does not.

⚖️ **It changes NO dollar and NO tax.** Line 28 is *"Add lines 8 through 27b"*, and both routes land
inside it — same total expenses, same net profit, same AGI, same self-employment tax.
⛔ **So this is a presentation defect, not a dollar defect**, and on its own it would not be worth
amending a closed year for.

🔴 **What it DOES change is that the return stops asking about the vehicle at all**, and that is the
real finding:

> **2024 Instructions, Line 9, *"Information on your vehicle"* — verbatim:**
> *"**If you claim any car and truck expenses, you must provide certain information on the use of
> your vehicle** by completing one of the following. **1.** Complete Schedule C, **Part IV**… **2.**
> Complete **Form 4562, Part V**…"*

🔑 **Read the trigger carefully: it is "if you claim ANY car and truck expenses" — not "if you use
line 9".** Routing the fuel through `Other expenses` does **not** escape the obligation. It only
removes the cue.
⚠️ **And the two texts are worded differently, which is recorded rather than leaned on:** the **instructions** key the duty to *claiming the expense*; the **form's Part IV
caption** keys it to *"claiming car or truck expenses **on line 9**"*. **Which governs a blank line 9 is a reading, not an IRS statement.** ⛔ **But do not read this as cover for 2024:** the captions only diverge *because* of the defect — the instruction requires the expense to be on line 9 in the first place, and the Part IV caption simply assumes it is.

**What went unanswered as a result** — Schedule C Part IV lines 43-47: the **date placed in
service**; the **business / commuting / other mileage** split; whether the vehicle was available for
personal use; whether another vehicle was available; and **"Do you have evidence to support your
deduction?"** with **"If Yes, is the evidence written?"**
🔑 **Those are answered under the Form 1040 jurat**, which covers *"this return and **accompanying
schedules and statements**"*. ⓘ *That the tick is therefore a statement under penalties of perjury
is a sound reading of those two texts — it is not a sentence the IRS prints.*

#### ③ The AMOUNT is the part nobody has tested — and it is the one with money in it

> **Pub. 463, *Actual Car Expenses*:** *"Actual car expenses include: Depreciation · Lease payments ·
> Registration fees · Licenses · Insurance · Repairs · **Gas** · Garage rent · Tires · Oil · Tolls ·
> Parking fees"*
> **And, immediately after — *Business and personal use*:** *"If you use your car for both business
> and personal purposes, **you must divide your expenses between business and personal use. You can
> divide your expense based on the miles driven for each purpose.**"* — with the worked example
> *"You can claim only 60% (12,000 ÷ 20,000) of the cost of operating your car."*

🔴 **The 2024 return deducted the fuel with no percentage anywhere.** Deducting all of it asserts
**100% business use of a car by a bartender**, which is a claim nobody made in writing and nobody
tested. ⚠️ **And 2024's fuel figure is more than DOUBLE that Schedule C's entire gross receipts** —
which is possible in a start-up year and is also exactly the shape that draws an eye.

> **Pub. 463, *How To Prove Expenses*:** *"**Note: You can't deduct amounts that you approximate or
> estimate.**"* · *"You should keep adequate records… You must generally prepare a **written
> record** for it to be considered adequate."*
> **Table 5-1, Transportation row:** *"For car expenses, **the cost of the car and any improvements,
> the date you started using it for business, the mileage for each business use, and the total miles
> for the year**."*

⛔ **The statute itself (§274(d)) could NOT be opened from here** — this environment's network policy
allows `irs.gov` only, and `ecfr.gov`, `uscode.house.gov` and `law.cornell.edu` are all refused. **So
the rule above is stated from Pub. 463, not from the Code**, and it is labelled that way on purpose.

#### ④ 🔴 THE PART THAT COSTS REAL MONEY IN 2025 — and it may already be unfixable

> 🔑 **2025 Instructions, Line 9 — this is the text that governs the 2025 return, verbatim:**
> *"You can take the standard mileage rate for **2025** **only if** you: • **Owned the vehicle and
> used the standard mileage rate for the first year you placed the vehicle in service**, or •
> Leased the vehicle and are using the standard mileage rate for the entire lease period."*
> ⓘ *(The 2024 instruction says the same for 2024 — it governs the 2024 facts, not this
> consequence.)* 🔑 **Read that way the chain is short: the rate is barred in 2025 unless it was
> USED in year one — and Pub. 463's Caution establishes that a return deducting gasoline did not
> use it.**
> **Pub. 463, *Choosing the standard mileage rate*:** *"If you want to use the standard mileage rate
> for a car you own, **you must choose to use it in the first year the car is available for use in
> your business**."* · *"You must make the choice… **by the due date (including extensions) of your
> return. You can't revoke the choice.**"*

🔑 **The two methods are exclusive** — Pub. 463's Caution: using the standard mileage rate you
*"can't deduct… **gasoline (including gasoline taxes)**, oil, insurance, or vehicle registration
fees."*

🛑 **So deducting fuel in 2024 was, in substance, an ACTUAL-EXPENSE claim.** The 2024 Schedule C
ticked box **H — *"started or acquired this business during 2024"*** — so 2024 is very likely the
first year the car was available for use in this business.

> ### ⚖️ BUT THE ANSWER IS NOT THE SAME FOR AN OWNED CAR AND A LEASED ONE, AND FOR AN OWNED CAR
> ### THERE IS A REAL ARGUMENT THE OTHER WAY. Do not state this as settled.
>
> 🔑 **Pub. 463 carries TWO separate rules and they are easy to collapse into one.** They are not
> the same rule and they do not reach the same answer here.
>
> | | The rule | What it does to him |
> |---|---|---|
> | **A** | ***"Choosing the standard mileage rate"*** — *"If you want to use the standard mileage rate for a car you own, you must choose to use it in the **first year the car is available for use in your business**."* | 🔴 **This is the one that bites an OWNED car.** If 2024 was that first year and the return claimed actual fuel, the rate is closed for that car |
> | **B** | ***"Standard mileage rate not allowed"*** — a **closed list**: five or more cars · depreciation by any method **other than straight line** · **MACRS** · **§179** · the **special depreciation allowance** · or *"Claimed actual car expenses after 1997 for a car you **leased**"* | ⚖️ **Read what is NOT on it.** For a car he **OWNS**, *"claimed actual car expenses"* is **not a disqualifier** — every owned-car entry on that list is **depreciation-based**, and **2024's line 13 was blank, so no depreciation of any kind was claimed** |
>
> ✅ **For a LEASED car the two rules agree and the answer is clear:** the standard mileage rate must
> be used *"for the entire lease period"*, **and** claiming actual car expenses on a leased car is
> on list B explicitly. **A 2024 actual-expense year rules it out for that lease.**
> ⚖️ **For an OWNED car they point different ways** — rule A says the election had to be made in year
> one; list B does not bar him. 🔑 **Rule A is the affirmative requirement and is the better reading**,
> but **the absence of his facts from list B is a genuine argument a reviewer will raise**, and it is
> recorded here so nobody has to rediscover it.
>
> ⛔ **AND THE LOGICALLY PRIOR QUESTION IS NOT SETTLED EITHER: whether the 2024 return made an
> ELECTION AT ALL.** It did not claim a car expense on line 9, gave no vehicle information, and
> identified nothing as a vehicle. 🔑 **A thin argument — but the whole point is that nobody can
> answer it without knowing what the car is.**
>
> 🛑 **So this paper does NOT conclude the rate is closed. It concludes the RISK is real and that it
> is the reason to ask now rather than in April** — and the position, once the facts are in, is
> **Lilian's and Julia's**, not a session's.
>
> ⚠️ **What IS settled either way: an amended 2024 return cannot create the election.** Pub. 463:
> *"You must make the choice to use the standard mileage rate **by the due date (including
> extensions) of your return. You can't revoke the choice.**"*

⚖️ **Why it matters in dollars: 2025's rate is 70 cents a mile** *(2024 was 67)*. For a caterer
driving to events, the mileage method is frequently worth **several times** a fuel-only deduction —
and it needs no receipts beyond a log.

#### ⑤ ⚠️ TWO THINGS THIS PAPER DOES NOT KNOW, AND MUST NOT ASSERT

1. 🔴 **Whether the 2024 return actually carried a Form 4562.** **Page 12 of 12 yielded the taxpayer's name and nothing else** *(§1 calls this "barely extracted"; the two phrasings mean the same thing)* — ⚠️ **and a name block is how every attachment form OPENS**, which is weak *positive* evidence that page 12 is a form rather than a blank
   *(§1 caveat)*, and **Form 4562's attachment sequence number is 179 — the highest in that
   package** *(Schedule C 09, Form 8995 55, Form 8962 73)*, so a printed return ordered by sequence
   would put a 4562 **exactly there**. ⛔ **The absence of vehicle information is therefore NOT
   established.** ✅ **What IS established, because those pages did extract:** Schedule C **Part IV
   is blank** and **line 13 (depreciation) is blank**, so no depreciation was claimed.
   🛠️ **Ten-second check: open the 2024 PDF at the last page.**
2. ⚠️ **Whether the fuel was CAR fuel at all.** Part V says only `GAS & FUEL`. On a **catering**
   business, fuel for equipment — a generator, warming units — is **not** a car expense, and would
   legitimately sit outside line 9. 🔑 **That reading would excuse the line, and it would also remove the PREMISE OF ④ entirely** —
   if no car expense was claimed in 2024 there was no actual-expense method to choose, and the
   method question reopens. ⛔ **It would not excuse the amount.** A question for the client, never
   an assumption.

#### ⑥ ✅ WHAT TO DO IN 2025 — the fix, in order

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Ask what the car actually is** | owned or leased · when it was first used for this business · was it the same car in 2024 |
| **2** | **Ask for the mileage** | **total miles** for 2025 and **business miles**, and whether a written log exists. Without them there is no defensible figure under either method |
| **3** | **Establish which method is even available** | 🔴 **If LEASED and 2024 claimed actual expenses, the mileage rate is out** — both Pub. 463 rules agree. ⚖️ **If OWNED, it is arguable and it is Lilian's and Julia's position to take** — see the two-rule table above. **Either way, model both before choosing** |
| **4** | **Put it on line 9** | 🔑 **the business portion only** — never the gross |
| **5** | **Complete the vehicle information — and check WHICH form, because the instructions add a condition the form's caption does not** | ⚠️ Part IV applies only if **(a)** you claim the standard mileage rate, **lease** the vehicle, or it is **fully depreciated**, **and (b)** no Form 4562 is required for another reason. 🔴 **On step 3's likely branch — owned, actual expenses, not yet depreciated — none of (a) holds and depreciation on a vehicle puts him on FORM 4562 PART V.** Decide it explicitly rather than defaulting to Part IV |
| **6** | 🔴 **Get the DEPRECIATION facts too — the fix list is incomplete without them** | Actual expenses means the car's **cost, any improvements, and the date it was placed in service** *(Pub. 463 Table 5-1 names exactly these)*. **Depreciation is usually the largest actual-expense item**, and ⚠️ **how it is claimed is a DECISION, not a mechanical step**: §179, the special allowance or MACRS each become a **permanent bar** on the mileage rate for that car going forward *(Pub. 463, "Standard mileage rate not allowed")* |
| **7** | **The phone** | It is not a vehicle cost, so §3E does not reach it. ⚠️ **But this paper has NOT tested it against the same caption**: line 25 is `Utilities`, and the line 25 instruction addresses telephone service directly — *"**Local telephone service.** If you used your home phone for business, do not deduct the base rate… But you can deduct any additional costs you incurred for business"*. **Untested, not endorsed** |
| **8** | ⓘ **2025 only — the vehicle loan interest** | The 2025 line 9 instruction prints a new CAUTION: a deduction claimed on **Schedule 1-A** for qualified passenger vehicle loan interest allocable to personal use **cannot also be claimed on Schedule C**. He will be on Schedule 1-A anyway for the tips deduction |

⛔ **Whether to amend 2024 is Lilian's and Julia's call, not this paper's.** The line placement alone
moved no tax; **the business-use percentage may have**, and that is the half worth weighing.

a group they ascend but across groups they do not. **Nothing is missing if a number looks skipped.**

      own text** says so on the face of the form. ⚠️ **§11F also records that the stronger regulation
      citation could NOT be verified; the service-charge exclusion stands on the instructions (p.102).**
      🥈 **SECOND:** ask **Compass Group** for a breakdown of the box-14 `TIPS` amount
      between **voluntarily-paid tips** and **distributed service charges / automatic gratuities**,
      because the second kind is **not** a qualified tip *(2025 Instructions for Form 1040 p. 102,
      and its own Example 1)*. ⚠️ **On a contract-catering employer this is the likely case, not the
      edge case**, and it is the largest single item on the return.
      🔑 **ASK IT PRECISELY, BECAUSE THE OBVIOUS WORDING GETS THE WRONG ANSWER** *(the p. 102
      definition, read 2026-09-13)*: ✅ **a TIP POOL qualifies — even a MANDATORY one.** Cash tips
      expressly include amounts received *"through a **mandatory or voluntary** tip-sharing
      arrangement, such as a **tip pool**"*. ⛔ **What disqualifies is a charge ADDED TO THE
      CUSTOMER'S BILL** — *"service charges, automatic gratuities, or any other mandatory amounts
      automatically added to a customer's bill by the vendor or the establishment, unless the
      customer is expressly provided an option to disregard or modify such charges … without
      consequence."* 🛑 **So "was it mandatory?" is the WRONG question and will produce a false
      negative** — a mandatory share-out of real tips still qualifies. **The right question is where
      the money originated: did the CUSTOMER choose to pay it, or did the invoice impose it?**
      **Then** run the multi-employer
      worksheet per employer, confirm each job was a listed tipped occupation, and ask employers 2
      and 3 for final pay stubs if either had tips that never reached a W-2 box.

🆕 🔴 **AND TWO THAT WERE NEVER ON ANY LIST — SCHEDULE 1-A HAS MORE THAN ONE PART**

- [ ] 🆕 🔴 **⑲ SCHEDULE 1-A PART III — NO TAX ON OVERTIME. Never considered anywhere, and it is the
      largest unexamined number on this return.** This return **already files Schedule 1-A**; the paper
      analysed **Part II and nothing else**. He works at **four catering, events and concessions
      employers** with **40,526 of W-2 wages**, where overtime is ordinary — ⛔ **though his pay basis
      and FLSA status are NOT established and are part of the same employer ask.**
      📖 **Read off the 2025 form: line 14a takes qualified overtime included in W-2 box 1; line 15 caps
      it at 12,500 (Single); line 17's threshold is 150,000, nowhere near him.** ⚠️ **2025 W-2s were NOT
      required to report overtime separately** — the same transition gap that hid the tips in box 14.
      🟢 **It is BELOW the line (1040 13b), so it cannot move the AGI, the 389%, the repayment cap or
      the 60,240 cliff** — all AGI-driven. ⛔ **BUT IT IS NOT A FREE TRADE: it shares Form 8995's
      line 11 base with the tips**, so above roughly **9,050** it starts clawing the QBI deduction
      back. 💵 **At the 12,500 ceiling that is a 690 clawback and the deduction is worth ≈1,380, not
      ≈1,500.** 🛠️ **Re-test the headroom (§9B: 1,810) against whatever the employers give.**
      ⛔ **And qualified overtime EXCLUDES qualified tips** *(2025 Instructions for Form 1040:
      "Qualified overtime compensation doesn't include any amount you receive as a qualified tip")* —
      **this return already claims tips from these same employers, so the two must not overlap.**
      🛠️ **ASK EACH EMPLOYER FOR THE FLSA OVERTIME *PREMIUM* — the "half" of time-and-a-half.**
      ⛔ **Not "total overtime pay", which is what a payroll clerk sends if the question is loose.**
      ⓘ *A pay register is enough: the instructions allow a reasonable method from the regular rate and
      hours over 40 in a workweek.* ⛔ **Record a "no overtime anywhere" as a finding — silence is not one.**

      **156** net of a 325 QBI clawback. ⛔ **Schedule 2 line 1a does NOT move** — line 27 falls to
      4,537 and the 1,625 cap still binds, so there is nothing to iterate. 🔴 **WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS
      THE ELIGIBILITY GATE, and it is an EMPLOYER question:** §162(l)(2)(B) denies it for any month he
      was **ELIGIBLE** — not enrolled — for a **subsidised** employer plan, across **four** employers.
      ⛔ **Do not read the twelve accepted PTC months as the answer: it is a different test (§11A).**

      ⓘ *It read, before it was computed:* 2024 could not raise this because the Schedule C ran at a
      **loss**. **2025 will run at a profit**, which makes the **§162(l) self-employed health
      insurance deduction** available on his Marketplace premiums — and that deduction and the
      premium tax credit are **circular**: the deduction lowers AGI, which raises the PTC, which
      lowers the deductible premium. The 2025 Instructions for Form 8962, page 18, send this case out
      of the form entirely: *"If you are self-employed and are claiming the self-employed health
      insurance deduction, see Self-Employed Health Insurance Deduction and PTC in **Pub. 974** for
      the amount to enter on line 28."* ⚠️ **Pub. 974 was NOT opened in this review.** Do it before
      computing Form 8962. ⓘ *(End of the archival text — **it WAS opened, §11A.**)*

**Not applicable.** This is an individual return and it feeds no other. He is **not** an owner of
Maxratings LLC — the operating agreements name other members entirely — so no K-1 arises from that
company, and the Delaware LLC's status is question ⑩.

⚠️ **The reverse dependency is worth naming:** three of his 1099-NEC payers are themselves JK
clients, so **the firm's own files can corroborate his income** — which is how the VoiceCapital 1099
surfaced at all, and then how it was **settled** a day later: it was found, and read, in the
**payer's** Drive folder rather than the client's (§3B).

### 9C · ✅ Two things this return got right that nothing in the 2025 documents would have prompted

🔑 **Say these first, because they are the ones a reviewer will not notice and they are the hard ones.**

1. ✅ **Form 8995 line 3 carries the (2,005) qualified-business-loss carryforward.** Nothing in the

#### 🛠️ THE ENTRY ROUTE — where this is actually TYPED in ATX, and why the box-14 field is a dead end

> 🕐 **EVERYTHING BELOW THIS HEADING WAS WRITTEN BEFORE THE FIGURE WAS KEYED** *(2026-09-13, midday)*.

> is done** *(§4 decision 8)*, **and where a line below says "the filed return shows 1,082" it means
> the return AS FIRST REVIEWED.**

🔑 **Established 2026-09-13 from the ATX `W2 (1040)` input screen itself** *(Lilian keyed Compass's
box 14 and asked why the Description dropdown has no `TIPS` option)*. **This is the half §9D was
missing: the amount above cannot be claimed from where it is typed.**

⛔ **BOX 14's `Descrip - 1` FEEDS NOTHING.** It is a free-text/lookup label beside the amount. **There
is no `TIPS` option to find, and its absence is not a problem to solve:** the IRS prescribes **no
codes for box 14 at all** — box 14 is literally *"Other"*, and what appears there is whatever the
employer chose to write *(General Instructions for Forms W-2 and W-3, read 2026-09-13)*. **Leaving
the descriptor blank changes no figure on the return.**

✅ **THE FIELDS THAT DO FEED SCHEDULE 1-A ARE HIGHER UP THE SAME SCREEN**, in a block ATX heads
**`Qualified Tips (No Tax on Tips)`**:

| On screen | What it says | Which case |
|---|---|---|
| ☐ **first checkbox** | *"Check `X` if Box 5 is $176,100 or LESS and Box 7 social security tips has an amount. **(Enter qualified tips under Box 7.)**"* | **Professional Concessions** — box 7 carries 1,082.38 |
| ☐ **second checkbox** | *"Check `X` if Box 7 is NOT applicable for ANY of the following reasons"* — and the second reason listed is **"Box 7 social Security tips is blank."** | 🔑 **COMPASS — this is the one that fits.** Box 7 is empty; the figure is in box 14 |
| **`Qualified tips (in Box 7)`** | A **separate dollar field directly beneath box 7**, distinct from box 7 itself | **This is where the qualified amount is keyed**, per the first checkbox's own parenthesis |

🔴 **TICKING THE BOX ALONE DOES NOTHING — IT REVEALS A FIELD, AND THAT FIELD IS WHERE THE NUMBER
GOES.** *(Established 2026-09-13: Lilian ticked it and the Due total did not move. **That is the
expected behaviour, not a bug.**)* Ticking the second checkbox makes a new line appear directly
beneath it:

> **When Box 7 is NOT applicable based on the selection above:**
> *Enter here the qualified tips you reported to this employer on Form(s) 4070 or any similar form.*
> *(DO NOT enter unreported tip income here. Instead, complete Form 4137)* → **[dollar field]**

⛔ **ATX does NOT pull the box-14 amount into it.** The checkbox opens the field; **the figure is
typed.** 🔑 **If the Due total does not move, the field is empty** — that is the whole diagnosis.

⚠️ **AND THAT FIELD'S LABEL IS NARROWER THAN THE IRS RULE — do not be stopped by it.** It names only
**Form 4070** *(the monthly report an employee makes to their employer under §6053)*, which this
client almost certainly does not have. **But the 2025 Instructions for Form 1040, p. 102-103, give
FOUR methods for figuring qualified tips, and the THIRD is this exact case, verbatim:**

> *"**3.** If your employer voluntarily chooses to report the amount of your tips **in box 14 of your
> Form W-2** (or on a separate statement), **you can use the amount reported to you**."*

✅ **So box 14 is a source in its own right, with no Form 4070 anywhere.** ⓘ *And the IRS says why on
the same page — it is the transition problem, in its own words:* **"Because no changes have been made
to Form W-2 for 2025, a separate accounting for cash tips you report to your employer may not appear
on your Form W-2 for 2025."** ⚠️ **Note one asymmetry worth knowing: methods 1, 2 and 4 each say
which line to enter the figure on; method 3 does not.** That is an omission in the instructions, not
a prohibition — **but it is why the software's own field is labelled for method 2.**
⛔ **The `DO NOT enter unreported tip income here` warning does NOT apply**: unreported tips are tips
the employee told nobody about. **These were reported — by the employer, on the W-2.**

🛑 **BUT TICKING THAT SECOND BOX IS NOT A DATA-ENTRY STEP — IT IS THE ELIGIBILITY ASSERTION §9D SAYS
IS NOT ESTABLISHED.** ⛔ **Do not tick it to make the screen behave.**

> 🆕 **OUTCOME: it WAS ticked, deliberately — §4 decision 8.** ⚠️ **The warning stands exactly as
> written: it is what makes decision 8 a POSITION rather than a keystroke, and it is why §6 item ④
> says to UNTICK it if the employer's answer goes the other way.**

ATX's own label says *"refer to
Form 1040 instructions"*, and §3D② is where those instructions land: **a distributed service charge
is not a qualified tip**, and **an empty box 7 beside a box-14 line marked `TIPS` is at least as
consistent with "these were never §6053-reported cash tips" as with "the employer used box 14
instead."** 🔑 **The question goes to COMPASS GROUP first (§6 item ④). The keystrokes are five

### 9E · ⚠️ 389% — AND THE CLIFF IS 1,563 DOLLARS AWAY

🔑 **This is the number nobody would look for, and it is the reason the missing Schedule C expenses
are worth more than they appear.**

His household income is **58,677**. **400% of the federal poverty line for a family of one is
60,240.** He is **1,563 dollars below the cliff.**

| Where he lands | Repayment cap | What he repays |
|---|---|---|
| **300–400%** *(45,180 – 60,239)* — **he is at 389%, as filed** | **1,625** | **1,625** |
| **400% or more** | 🔴 **NONE — the cap disappears** | 🔴 **5,121 — and note the excess GROWS, it is not the same 4,830** |
| **200–300%** *(30,120 – 45,179)* | **975** | 975, **and the allowed PTC rises too** |
| Under **200%** *(< 30,120)* | **375** | *(cannot bite on these facts — listed so the table is not read as complete)* |

🛑 **THE 400% ROW IS NOT "the same excess, uncapped" — AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS TABLE GOT THAT
WRONG.** Crossing the threshold moves the **applicable figure from 0.0823 to 0.0850**, which **raises
the contribution amount and shrinks the allowed credit**, so the excess itself grows. At exactly
60,240: line 8a = 60,240 × 0.0850 = **5,120**; allowed PTC = 5,865 − 5,120 = **745** *(not 1,036)*;
line 27 = 5,866 − 745 = **5,121**, uncapped.

🔴 **So income that turns up later — §6 item ⑮'s disappeared 2024 payer is the live candidate — does
not cost him its marginal tax. The swing is 5,121 − 1,625 ≈ 3,500 dollars the moment it crosses
60,240**, on top of the ordinary income tax and SE tax on the extra income itself.
⚠️ **Conversely the Schedule C expenses push him AWAY from the cliff.** ⛔ **But reaching the 200–300%
band would take roughly 14,500 of expenses against 19,531 of gross receipts, and nothing in the file
suggests that** — §9F's own estimate is "thousands, not hundreds". **The gain that is realistic is
distance from the cliff, not a change of band.**

the completed Profit & Loss template, so no expenses are reported. **That is a decision, not an
oversight, and it is documented — which is exactly right.**

⚠️ **What the note does not say, and what the reviewer should see, is that the cost COMPOUNDS** — the
same figure is wrong in four places at once, and 9E is why the fourth is the expensive one:

1. Schedule C profit **overstated** → 2. SE tax **overstated** *(15.3% of 92.35%)* → 3. AGI
**overstated** *(the profit, less only half the SE tax)* → 4. 🔴 **the FPL percentage overstated**,
which is what sets the repayment cap.

🔑 **2024's were fuel 2,080 and phone 870 — 2,950 against gross receipts of 945.** A comparable year
would move the AGI by thousands, not hundreds. **Chasing that template is the highest-value hour left
on this return**, and §3E's vehicle questions have to be answered before any fuel figure is used.

## 10 · 📋 THE NOTE FOR JULIA — the accumulating draft

> 🔑 **WHY THIS SECTION EXISTS.** _(Lilian, 2026-09-13: "cuando terminemos, hagamos una nota para
> Julia con todos los comentarios de los análisis que hicimos en esta declaración… en resumen, este
> análisis tenemos que tomarlo como una nota.")_ **Considerations arrive one at a time, days apart,
> while the return is being keyed. The note goes out ONCE, at the end.** ⛔ **Reconstructing it from
> memory on the last day is how the reasoning gets lost** — which is the gap
> [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §4C exists to close: *Julia reviews it
> afterwards, and was not in the room.*
> ✅ **So each consideration is drafted HERE, in the shape Double needs, on the day it happens.** When
> the return is finished the bullets are appended to the existing note and nothing is re-derived.
> ⛔ **This is a DRAFT, not the note.** The live note is Double **524890**; only bullets marked
> ✅ PUBLISHED are actually in it.

### 10A · Where it lives, and the rules that bind it

| | |
|---|---|
| **The note** | Double note **524890** — `2025 Form 1040 — preparation notes for review`, on client `710663`. [Deep link](https://app.doublehq.com/clients/710663/info/notes/524890) |
| **One note, rewritten in place** | ⛔ **Never a second note on this matter** — [`double-mcp`](../../../.claude/skills/double-mcp/) §7 rule 1. `list_notes` first, edit the body you fetched, never re-author from memory |
| **Byline** | ✅ **Already on the note** — the connector posts under Julia's login, so without it the trail is anonymous. ⛔ **Do NOT re-author it from the skill's template.** Double's editor rewrote `<em>` to `<i>` when someone saved the note in the UI, so the live markup is `<p><i>Written by </i><strong><i>Lilian Gonzalez</i></strong></p>`. 🔑 **Fetch the body and edit THAT** — re-authoring silently reverts a person's own save |
| **Where a new bullet goes** | **Inside the existing `<ul>`, after the last `<li>`** — never after `</ul>`, and never as a second list |
| **Footer** | `<p><i>Last updated: YYYY-MM-DD</i></p>` is the last element and **its date is refreshed on every edit** *(§7 rule 5)* |
| **Language** | **English**, like every firm artifact, whatever language the session ran in |
| **Size** | Keep the whole body under **~7,500 characters** — above roughly 8,000 the request is refused outright, not truncated (§7's size wall) |
| **⚠️ Rule 11 and its carve-out** | A note normally carries **the client's information, not our analysis**. 🔑 **Lilian authorised analysis in THIS note by name** *(2026-09-13)*, which is precisely the case-by-case exception rule 11 provides for. ⛔ **It does not generalise to other notes** |
| **Rule 12's test, on every line** | *Does this change the return, or tell someone what to do?* If neither, it belongs in this working paper or the client file instead |

### 10C · ✅ THE BLOCK THAT CAUGHT A WRONG STATEMENT TO THE SIGNER — resolved, and worth keeping

🛑 **The a.m. draft of bullet ④ said *"I did not claim it"*.** That was true of the return as first
reviewed — §9D, §4 decision 5, §6 item ④ unticked, §9I recording the worksheet was never run.

⚠️ **But the preparer was keying ATX while the bullet was being drafted, and described the intention
as *"tomamos que estas propinas eran calificadas y la incluimos"*.** ⛔ **Whether the figure had
actually been keyed after the reviewed PDF was produced was NOT established — so the bullet was
HELD rather than guessed at**, with the resolution written down as a single check: the `Due` box.

✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-13 (p.m.).** A second PDF arrived, was read through the redactor, and every
figure re-verified: **Due 4,802**, Schedule 1-A **4c = 17,730**, 1040 **13b = 17,730**. **It had been
keyed.** The recorded order was then followed exactly:

1. ✅ **The working paper was corrected FIRST** — §9B's moved rows, §9D rewritten around the position
   taken, **§4 decision 8** created, and **§6 item ④ reversed in posture** from a gate into a
   liability. **The objection travels with the decision**, per CLAUDE.md.
2. ✅ **Then bullet ④ was rewritten** to report what the return actually carries, and posted.

🔑 **KEEP THIS PATTERN.** A note drafted while someone is still at the keyboard **goes stale between
the drafting and the posting**, and nothing in the note itself shows it. ⛔ **The failure mode is
silent: the reviewer is simply told the opposite of what the return does.** ✅ **The fix is cheap —
name the ONE number that distinguishes the two states, and hold the bullet until someone looks at
it.**

⚠️ **ONE HONEST LIMIT ON THAT PATTERN, because a single number is not always a clean binary.** Here a
**third state existed** that the `Due` box could not see: the eligibility **checkbox ticked with the
amount field still empty** — which is how the return stood for part of the afternoon. ⛔ **The Due
reads 6,800 in that state, identical to "nothing was done", while an unsupported eligibility
assertion sits on the return.** 🔑 **So name the number, but ask what ELSE the number cannot
distinguish** — here it is §6 item ④'s untick-it-too instruction.

### 10E · What was deliberately CUT from bullet ④, so nobody re-adds it by accident

| Cut | Why |
|---|---|
| **The ATX entry route** *(the checkbox, the revealed field, where the figure is typed)* | Rule 12's second axis is **our own process commentary** — *"how our tools behaved is our business, not the return's."* It has a designated home: the **entry route is a required column of the working paper** *(`tax-return-sop` §4B)*, and **§9D holds it in full** |
| **Both citations** *(Notice 2025-69 §III.A.1 · 2025 Instructions p. 102-103 method 3)* | They support the **reporting route**, which is the **settled** half — rule 12 cuts the derivation behind a fact the reader will simply accept. ⚠️ **The DISQUALIFYING rule is the open half and it stays** — carried in plain words *(bill-imposed vs customer-chosen)* rather than as a citation |
| **The multi-employer mechanics** *(4a/4b to `-0-`, 4c off the worksheet)* | 🛑 **The rationale as written is now DEAD and is kept only as the record:** ~~*nothing changes until Compass answers, and for the one-employer case 4a = 4c = 1,082 is correct as filed*~~ — **the figure was keyed without Compass answering, and the return carries 4a/4b blank with 4c = 17,730.** ✅ **The cut still stands on its own second leg:** the mechanics are software behaviour, they resolved themselves, and **§9D's checkpoint table is where whoever keys a figure will actually be standing** |
| **A per-bullet signature** | The note already carries the byline, and the three published bullets carry none. Adding one to the fourth is the plainest *"different hand"* tell |

⚠️ **Three things were ADDED against the drafter's first pass, and each is load-bearing:**
**the provenance hedge** *(that W-2 was read off a scan and its box 14 has no corroboration — §3A
row 1, §4 decision 2; rule 9 requires the master's caveats to travel)*; **the occupation leg**
*(if the Compass role was not a listed tipped occupation the whole amount is out regardless of the
service-charge answer — §3D② runs the occupation filter first)*; and **the wording warning**
*(a mandatory tip pool still qualifies, so "was it mandatory?" produces a false negative and would
throw the deduction away for a reason the instructions expressly reject)*.

### 11A · ✅ ITEM ⑯ IS NOW A COMPUTATION — the §162(l) deduction is **1,625**, and the circle **resolves in one pass**

🔑 **The input §9I called missing was already in this paper.** Pub. 974's Worksheet W line 1 wants
*specified premiums*; **§9A has them** — Form 1095-A column **A** annual **5,865.60**, column **C**
(APTC) **5,865.60**. **No client document had to be opened for this.**

ⓘ *Worksheet W lines 1, 2, 3, 14 and 15 and Worksheet X lines 25, 26, 28 and 30 were read off the
Pub. 974 (2025) PDF on 2026-09-17 and are quoted from it; the Part III cascade below is reproduced
from the same document.*

| Pub. 974 (2025) | Line | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Worksheet W 1 | specified premiums *(1095-A col A)* | **5,865.60** |
| Worksheet W 2 | APTC *(1095-A col C)* | **5,865.60** |
| Worksheet W 3 | line 1 − line 2 | **0** — he bore nothing out of pocket in-year |
| Worksheet W 15 | earned-income limit *(19,531 − 1,380)* | 18,151 |
| Worksheet X 25 | repayment limitation | **1,625** |
| Worksheet X 26 | W-16 + X-25 = 0 + 1,625 | 1,625 |
| Worksheet X 28 | smaller of 26 or W-1 | 1,625 |
| Worksheet X 30 | smaller of 28 or W-15 | 🔴 **1,625** |

⌨️ **Schedule 1 (Form 1040), Part II, line 17 = `1625` — TYPED.**
✅ **Both of Pub. 974's methods converge on the same 1,625 here**, so the method choice is neutral.
🛑 **AND THERE ARE TWO WORKSHEETS CALLED "STEP 3 WORKSHEET" — one per method, DIFFERENT LENGTHS.**
⚠️ **This is a real trap and it caught this pass:** an earlier version of this section read the
Iterative one and labelled it Simplified. **Read off Pub. 974 (2025), 2026-09-17:**

| Method | Its Step 3 Worksheet | The deduction is |
|---|---|---|
| **Iterative** *(p.53)* | ends at **line 9** — *"smaller of line 7 or line 8. **Then, go to Step 4 next**"* | **line 9 + Worksheet W line 14** *(p.54 says so in terms)* |
| **Simplified** *(p.55)* | runs to **line 11** | **line 11** = line 9 + line 10, where **line 10 = Worksheet W line 14** |

📖 **Worksheet W line 14 (p.50), verbatim:** *"Enter your self-employed health insurance deduction for
**nonspecified premiums** from Worksheet P, line 3, or Form 7206, line 14."*
🔑 **So both methods have the same shape — specified-premium deduction PLUS nonspecified — and they
agree at 1,625 here only because Worksheet W line 14 is ZERO.** ⛔ **That is exactly why the
dental/vision/LTC question below matters: Worksheet P → Worksheet W line 14 → Step 3 line 10 is the
ROUTE by which any such premium reaches Schedule 1 line 17.**
ⓘ *Line 9 itself is "the smaller of line 7 or line 8": line 7 = specified premiums 5,865.60 less the
Step 2 PTC apportioned by month (here 1,329) = 4,536.60; line 8 = Worksheet X line 30 = 1,625.*
⚠️ *The Iterative method carries a usability condition — usable only "if you can complete Step 6",
i.e. if the changes converge under $1.00.*
ⓘ **AND ONE INPUT IS MORE GENEROUS THAN IT LOOKS:** the Step 3 Worksheet's own note reads
*"Self-employment for part of a month counts as a full month of self-employment."*

🔑 **AND THE ANSWER ONLY COMES OUT AT 1,625 BECAUSE COLUMN A EQUALS COLUMN C.** He bore **nothing**
out of pocket in-year, so Worksheet W line 3 is zero and the only premium he is treated as paying is
the repayment. ⛔ **Do not carry "the deduction equals the repayment limitation" to another client** —
it is a coincidence of this fact pattern, not a rule. **Every dollar of column A above the APTC would
add a dollar to Worksheet X line 26.**

🛠️ **ONE INPUT STILL TO CONFIRM: any DENTAL, VISION or LONG-TERM-CARE premium paid separately.**
Those are **nonspecified premiums** — Pub. 974 handles them through **Worksheet P** (or Form 7206)
rather than Worksheets W and X, **which carry specified Marketplace premiums only**. **Nothing in this
file says whether he paid any**, and they would be additional to the 1,625.

🟢 **AND THE ANSWER IS MORE ROBUST THAN THE OPEN GATE SUGGESTS — but the test is line 9, not the cap.**
⛔ **An earlier version of this table read Worksheet X line 28 alone and was WRONG below five months**,
because it skipped the Step 3 line 7 test *(specified premiums **less the PTC apportioned by month**)*.
Corrected:

| Months surviving the §162(l) gate | Specified premiums | line 7 *(less apportioned PTC)* | X-30 *(cap)* | **line 9 = DEDUCTION** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **12 … 5** | 5,865.60 → 2,444.00 | 4,536.60 → 1,890.25 | 1,625 | 🟢 **1,625 — unchanged** |
| 4 | 1,955.20 | **1,512.20** | 1,625 | **1,512.20** |
| 3 | 1,466.40 | **1,134.15** | 1,466.40 | **1,134.15** |
| 2 | 977.60 | 756.10 | 977.60 | 756.10 |
| 1 | 488.80 | 378.05 | 488.80 | 378.05 |

🔑 **So the employer answer only changes the figure if it knocks out EIGHT OR MORE of the twelve
months** — ⓘ *the crossover is at five, not four.* ⓘ *The sub-5 rows hold the PTC fixed at 1,329
while it would in fact drift with the deduction; re-running the 4-month row with its own PTC gives
line 7 = 1,522.87, still under the cap — **the crossover survives, the approximation does not bite**.* ⚠️ **It can still take the deduction to ZERO**
*(eligible in every month → no deduction at all)* — **but there is no partial answer between 5 and 12
that changes it.**

🟢 **AND THIS IS WHAT MAKES IT EASY — BUT SAY IT CORRECTLY, BECAUSE THE TEMPTING VERSION IS WRONG.**
⛔ **The feedback loop is NOT "severed".** It closes through the **repayment-limitation BRACKET**,
which is a function of household income, which is a function of the deduction. 🔑 **Worksheet X
Part III lines 15–24 IS the IRS's own resolution of exactly that circularity** — it cascades through
the three brackets in turn instead of reading Table 5 once:

| Worksheet X | Subtract | Household income | % FPL | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| l.15–18 | min(W-19, **375**) = 375 | 58,302 | **387%** | ≥200 → continue |
| l.19–21 | min(W-19, **975**) = 975 | 57,702 | **383%** | ≥300 → continue |
| l.22–25 | min(W-19, **1,625**) = 1,625 | 57,052 | **378%** | <400 → **line 25 = 1,625** |

✅ **It lands in the SAME 300–400% band both times, which is why no iteration is needed HERE** — not
because the loop cannot close in principle. ⓘ *W-19 is Worksheet W line 15 minus line 16 = 18,151 − 0.*

**And the repayment itself does not move:**

| | As filed | With the 1,625 deduction |
|---|---|---|
| 1040 11a AGI | 58,677 | **57,052** |
| 8962 5 (FPL %) | 389% | **378%** |
| 8962 7 applicable figure | 0.0823 | **0.0795** |
| 8962 24 allowed PTC | 1,036 | **1,329** |
| 8962 27 excess APTC | 4,830 | **4,537** |
| 8962 28 cap · **29 / Sch 2 1a** | 1,625 · **1,625** | 1,625 · 🔑 **1,625 — UNCHANGED** |

⛔ **Line 27 never comes near the cap**, so the repayment does not move. **The extra 293 of allowed
PTC is dead weight — it only shrinks the excess he keeps without repaying, which costs him nothing.**

🛑 **THE BOUND, AND IT PROVES THE POINT ABOUT THE BRACKET.** Push the deduction to its theoretical
maximum — the full **18,151** earned-income limit — and AGI falls to **40,526 = 269%** of FPL. There
the applicable figure is **0.0476**, the contribution 1,929, the allowed PTC 3,936 and line 27
**1,930** — ⚠️ **but the Table 5 cap at 269% is 975, not 1,625, so Schedule 2 line 1a WOULD fall to
975, a 650 move.** 🔑 **It cannot actually happen, because Worksheet X caps the deduction at 1,625
long before the deduction could get there** — which is the whole reason Part III cascades.
⛔ **So "the repayment is unmoved" is a SCOPED result about these figures, not a structural one.**

**What it is worth, end to end:**

| 1040 line | As filed | With the deduction |
|---|---|---|
| 11a AGI | 58,677 | 57,052 |
| 13a QBI *(8995 l.2 16,526 → l.4 14,521 → l.5)* | 3,229 | **2,904** — ⚠️ a 325 clawback |
| 14 | 36,709 | 36,384 |
| 15 taxable | 21,968 | **20,668** |
| 16 tax *(Tax Table)* | 2,399 | **2,243** |
| 24 total tax | 6,784 | **6,628** |
| **37 owed** | 4,802 | 🟢 **4,646** |

🔑 **Net saving 156** — ⚠️ **modest, and §9H item 3 overstated it by roughly 3.6×** by sizing it at
*"~5,866 of premiums"*. **The premiums are not the deduction**: the APTC covered 100% of them, so the
only premium he is treated as paying is the **1,625 he must repay.** ✅ **Corrected here.**

🛑 **ONE CHECK ON THE FORM, AND IT IS RIGHT ONLY BY COINCIDENCE HERE.** Pub. 974 says to put
**Worksheet X line 25** on Form 8962 line 28 and *"Do not enter an amount from Table 5 in the Form
8962 instructions."* At 378% both give **1,625**, so ATX's automatic Table 5 lookup lands correctly —
⚠️ **but had the Worksheet X percentages fallen below 300, the two would diverge and ATX would be
wrong. Verify line 28 rather than assume it.**

#### 🔴 THE GATE — and it is NOT the test Form 8962 already passed

⛔ **Do not read the twelve accepted PTC months as evidence that no employer plan existed. They are
different tests and both can be true at once.**

| | §162(l)(2)(B) — the deduction | Form 8962 — the credit |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger | **ELIGIBILITY**, not enrolment — *"even if you didn't actually participate"* *(2025 Instructions for **Form 7206**, "Other coverage")* | eligibility **only if** the offer was **affordable** *(≤9.02% of household income)* **and** met **minimum value** |
| Granularity | **month by month**; eligible for *any part* of a month kills that whole month | month by month |
| Also caught | a **QSEHRA** counts as a subsidised employer plan | Marketplace-determination safe harbour can preserve the credit |

🔑 **So an employer plan can destroy the deduction while leaving the credit intact.** ⚠️ **9.02% of
58,677 is 5,293 a year — about 441 a month**; an offer costing him more than that is unaffordable for
PTC purposes **and still fatal to the §162(l) deduction.**

🛠️ **THE ASK — to the EMPLOYERS, not to him**, month by month for 2025, for each of the four:
1. Did you maintain a group health plan he **could** have joined in 2025 — **whether or not he did**?
2. **Which months** was he eligible? *(Give eligibility start/end dates; a bona fide **waiting period**
   is not eligibility.)*
3. Did the employer **pay any part** of the premium? ⛔ **A plan where the employee pays 100% is not
   subsidised** and does not bite.
4. Was there a **QSEHRA**?

📄 **The shortcut: request Form 1095-C and read lines 14, 15 and 16 month by month.** A national
food-service group of Compass Group's size is almost certainly an Applicable Large Employer.

⚠️ **If he was eligible in all twelve months, the return as prepared is RIGHT and line 17 stays
blank.** **This is a confirm-or-claim, not a defect.**

🔴 **AND THE SAME QUESTION REACHES FURTHER THAN LINE 17 — this is the part that is easy to miss.**
An employer offer that was **affordable and met minimum value** does not merely kill the deduction:
under §36B it makes those months **non-coverage months**, which **removes the PTC for them and
rewrites Form 8962 itself**, not just Schedule 1. ⓘ *On these figures it still would not move the
2025 bottom line — line 24 would fall to 0 and line 27 rise to 5,866, but **line 28 caps it at 1,625
either way**.* ⚠️ **Where it WOULD bite is §9E's cliff scenario: at 400% of FPL line 28 is blank and
the whole excess is repaid, so this fact makes that 3,500 swing LARGER, not smaller.**
🔑 **So ask it once and record it — it is a gate on the deduction, on the credit, and on the cliff.**

🛑 **This return already files Schedule 1-A. This paper analysed Part II and nothing else** — §3D②
is headed *"Part II — No Tax on Tips"*, §9B has no Part III row, and **§9I did not list it among what
the review did not do** *(this pass added it there)*. ⛔ **It is neither claimed nor ruled out.**

✅ **Read off the 2025 Schedule 1-A PDF (irs.gov, 2026-09-17):**

| Line | What | |
|---|---|---|
| 14a | *"Qualified overtime compensation included in Form W-2, box 1"* | ⌨️ **TYPED** |
| 14b | same from 1099-NEC box 1 / 1099-MISC box 3 | ⌨️ TYPED |
| 14c | 14a + 14b | COMPUTED |
| **15** | *"Enter the smaller of the amount on line 14c or **$12,500**"* *(25,000 MFJ)* | COMPUTED |
| 16 / 17 | Part I line 3 *(MAGI)* / **150,000** threshold | COMPUTED / preprinted |
| 21 | → line 38 → **1040 line 13b** | COMPUTED |

🔑 **WHY IT IS LIVE, NOT SPECULATIVE.** He works at **four catering, events and concessions
employers** with **40,526 of W-2 wages**, where hourly FLSA-covered work is the norm.
⛔ **HIS PAY BASIS AND FLSA STATUS ARE NOT ESTABLISHED** — §3A has W-2 box figures and employer names,
nothing about how he is paid — and the 2025 instructions make it an employer question in terms:
*"Ask your employer or other service recipients whether you are an FLSA-eligible employee."*
**It rides on the same ask.** Overtime at banquet and event
employers is ordinary, not exceptional. ⚠️ **And 2025 W-2s were NOT required to report it
separately**, which is exactly why nothing in the paperwork raised it — **the same transition problem
that hid the tips in box 14.**

🟢 **It is BELOW the line** *(1040 line 13b, inside line 14)*, so it **cannot move the AGI, the 389%,
the repayment cap or the 60,240 cliff** — all AGI-driven.
🛑 **⛔ BUT IT IS NOT "a clean trade with no circularity", AND THE TIPS ARE WHY.** Line 13b is the
**same base §11E quotes**: Form 8995 **line 11 = line 11a − 12e − 13b**. The tips already consumed
most of the headroom — **§9B measures what is left at 1,810 over the 3,229 component** — so any Part
III deduction above roughly **9,050** starts clawing QBI back.
💵 **At the 12,500 ceiling: 8995 line 11 → 12,697, line 14 → 2,539, so the QBI deduction falls
3,229 → 2,539 — a 690 clawback — and the deduction is worth ≈1,380, not ≈1,500.** ⚠️ **Re-test the
headroom against whatever figure the employers actually give.**
⛔ **AND ONE EXCLUSION THAT BITES ON THIS RETURN SPECIFICALLY:** *"Qualified overtime compensation
doesn't include any amount you receive as a qualified tip"* — **and 17,730 of qualified tips from
these same employers is already claimed.** **The two must not overlap.**

🛠️ **THE ASK, AND THE WORDING MATTERS:** ask each employer for the **FLSA overtime *premium*** — the
**"half" portion of time-and-a-half**, i.e. the excess over the regular rate — ⛔ **not total overtime
pay**, which is the figure a payroll clerk will send if the question is loose. ⓘ *The instructions
also allow a reasonable method built from the regular rate and hours over 40 in a workweek, so a pay
register is enough even where payroll cannot split it.*

⛔ **Record the answer either way.** A "no overtime at any of the four" is a finding; silence is not.

### 11D · ✅ THE **93** PENALTY IS VERIFIED — and §9I's description of how is wrong

✅ **Recomputed from scratch off the 2025 Form 2210: 94.23.** The one-dollar difference is a rounding
/ prior-year artifact. **The 93 stands — leave the line alone.**

⛔ **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS PAPER.**
1. 🔴 **THE FORM 2210 "SHORT METHOD" NO LONGER EXISTS.** ✅ **Checked on the 2025 Form 2210 PDF itself
   (irs.gov, 2026-09-17): the words "Short Method" do not appear anywhere on the form.** It carries
   **Part I** *(Required Annual Payment)*, **Part II** *(Reasons for Filing)*, **Part III** *(Penalty
   Computation — Section A figures the underpayment, Section B the penalty)* and **Schedule AI**.
   ⛔ **§9I's *"plausible on a short-method estimate"* describes a form that is not there.**
2. ⚠️ **"Total underpayment 4,099 − 2,075 = 2,024" is not the base the current form uses** — the
   worksheet charges **each quarterly instalment separately from its own due date.**

✅ **A LATE-FILED PRIOR YEAR STILL SUPPORTS THE SAFE HARBOUR.** The 2024 return was e-filed
**2025-12-01**, after three of the four 2025 instalment dates had passed. The condition in the 2025
Instructions for Form 2210 is only that a return was **filed** and covered **12 months** — ⛔ **there
is no timeliness condition anywhere in the form, its instructions, or the 1040 line 38 exception.**

🔑 **AND THE CONCLUSION THAT MATTERS FOR EVERYTHING ELSE UNDER REVIEW: nothing on the DEDUCTION side
moves this penalty.** ⚠️ **A change to the WITHHOLDING would** — the base is *required annual payment
less withholding* — **which is why §6 item ㉑ is not cosmetic.**
The required annual payment is the **smaller** of 90% of 2025's tax (6,106) and **100% of 2024's
(4,099)** — 2024 governs, and 2024 is closed. **So the tips deduction, the §162(l) deduction, the QBI
carryforward, the zero Schedule C expenses and the 8962 repayment all leave the 93 exactly where it
is.** *(110% does not apply: 2024 AGI 42,787 is below 150,000.)*

client figures by design**, which is what §4B asks for. ⚠️ **Deleting it when the return is filed is
right and does not undo a forward that has already happened** — that is the property that makes it
point 1 rather than point 3.

### 12F · ⓘ ONE FALSE ALARM, RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-CHASES IT

A `250` appears on Schedule 1 page 2 and the **blank 2025 form has no preprinted `250` anywhere on
that page**, which made it look like an unexplained adjustment — an error that would have moved the
AGI. ✅ **It is not one.** The PDF extraction **concatenates a line number with its value**: `250` is
**line 25 = 0**, exactly as `261,380` is **line 26 = 1,380** and `14c0` is **line 14c = 0**.
🔑 **The arithmetic was the check that settled it** — 60,057 − 58,677 = 1,380, so line 26 can only be
1,380. ⚠️ **Worth carrying to any future read of an ATX PDF through the redactor: a "value" that
begins with a line number probably IS one.**

## 13 · 🧾 THE CLIENT'S EXPENSE WORKBOOK — 2026-09-18

> 🔑 **What arrived.** Lilian: *"el cliente subio esto para sus gastos de negocio… creo que tiene otro
> trabajo del tipo sch C sobre credit score repair, pero tengo que puntualizarlo con el."*
> **A spreadsheet the client had built for us**, `2025_Tax_Prep_FINAL_Accountant_Package.xlsx`:
> 8 sheets, **1,519 extracted rows**, from **Capital One 360 checking, Capital One Venture and
> Quicksilver, Apple Card and Chase Prime Visa**.
> ✅ **Her credit-repair instinct is CONFIRMED by the file itself** — §13C.
> 🛑 **But the file's biggest effect is on the INCOME side, not the expense side, and it is the
> opposite of what it looks like — §13B.**

### 13A · What this file IS, and what it is NOT

**It says so itself** *(README sheet, verbatim)*: *"Prepare uploaded bank/card activity for accountant
review; **this is not a filed tax return**."* Every judgement in it is flagged back to us:
*"Accountant chooses mileage vs actual expenses and business-use %"*, *"Add business purpose/attendees"*,
*"eligibility/allocation must be established"*.

🛑 **AND IT HAS NO BUSINESS-PURPOSE COLUMN.** It records that money left an account and what the bank
called the payee. It nowhere records **why**. That is the exact defect the
[`personal-card-reimbursement`](../../../.claude/skills/personal-card-reimbursement/) skill's §0 is
built around — *a bank description is not a business purpose* — so **this file can be TRIAGED and
cannot be CATEGORISED**, and its own numbers say the same thing:

| The workbook's own totals | |
|---|---|
| Total outflow pooled | **83,563.46** |
| *of which* **"high-confidence deduction candidates"** | 🔴 **490.08 — 0.59%** |
| *of which* **"review pool — expenses needing documentation"** | **77,395.81** |
| *of which* "personal / nondeductible identified" | 5,677.57 |

⛔ **Nothing in the 77,395.81 is a deduction yet.** Read the 490.08 as the file's own honest estimate
of what it can support unaided.

### 13B · 🔴 THE MOST DANGEROUS THING IN THE FILE: **W-2 WAGES ALREADY ON THE RETURN ARE SITTING IN THE "UNCLASSIFIED INFLOW" POOL**

The workbook classifies **40,610.80 of inflows as "Unclassified inflow — confirm revenue,
reimbursement, transfer, gift, or personal"**, and a separate sheet — the client's **own** confirmations —
labels two of those payers **"Work income / Business income"**.

🔑 **They are two of the four W-2 employers already on this return.** The mechanism is a one-word rule:

| Bank description | Tool's verdict | Reality |
|---|---|---|
| `COMPASS GROUP **PAYROLL**` | W-2 / payroll ✅ | W-2 — §3A row 1 |
| `DO & CO Events U **PAYROLL**` | W-2 / payroll ✅ | W-2 — §3A row 3 |
| `CREATIVE EDGE PA **PAYROLL**` *(6 rows, 3,038.10)* | W-2 / payroll ✅ | W-2 — §3A row 2 |
| 🔴 `CREATIVE EDGE PA **DIRECT DEP**` *(17 rows, **9,579.58**)* | ❌ **Unclassified inflow** | **the SAME employer** |
| 🔴 `PROFESSIONAL CON **DIRECT DEP**` *(6 rows, **1,386.93**)* | ❌ **Unclassified inflow** | **W-2 — §3A row 4** |

⚠️ **The tool keyed on the literal word `PAYROLL`.** Creative Edge changed its payroll descriptor
mid-year — the last `DIRECT DEP` is **5 June**, the first `PAYROLL` is **25 September** — and the
client's own sheet then confirmed both payers as *"Work income"*, because to him **every one of these
is money he earned by working**. ⛔ **"Work income" in his vocabulary is NOT a statement about which
schedule a dollar belongs on**, and this sheet must never be read as if it were.

🧮 **THE TIE-OUT — Professional Concessions is exact to the penny:**

| From §3A, W-2 row 4 | |
|---|---|
| Box 1 wages | 1,539.22 |
| less box 2 federal w/h | (34.54) |
| less box 4 social security | (95.43) |
| less box 6 Medicare | (22.32) |
| **= net pay** | 🟢 **1,386.93** |
| **Deposits in the workbook** | 🟢 **1,386.93** |

**Creative Edge ties too, less exactly:** box 1 **13,990.77** less 652.57 + 867.43 + 202.87 =
**12,267.90** net, against **12,617.68** of deposits. ⓘ *The gap is 349.78, and the year's first
deposit is **355.55 on 2 January** — almost certainly a **2024** pay period. Excluding it leaves
12,262.13 against 12,267.90, within 5.77.* ⚠️ **That last step is a reading, not a record.**

> 🛑 **SO: about 10,966.51 of the 40,610.80 "unclassified" pool — 27% of it — corresponds to W-2 pay
> ALREADY ON THIS RETURN.** ⚠️ **Of that, 10,616.73 is established and 349.78 is unreconciled** *(the
> Creative Edge gap above, whose explanation this paper labels a reading rather than a record)* — so
> "at least 10,966.51", as an earlier draft had it, claimed more than the tie-out supports.
> 🔑 **Either way none of it is Schedule C income:** adding any of it there would report the same wages
> twice **and** convert them into self-employment income. ⛔ **Nothing from the unclassified pool goes on Schedule C until
> its payer is named and checked against §3A's four employers.**

(credit bureaus — that is Experian) and not 561440 (collection agencies)**; **real estate → `531210`

### 13D · How many Schedule Cs — and what actually turns on it

🛑 **The IRS rule is one Schedule C per business** — *2025 Instructions, page 3, Line A:*
*"If you owned more than one business, complete a separate Schedule C for each business."*
⛔ **But nothing in the 19 pages defines when two activities ARE one business.** It is a question of
**fact only the client can answer**, which is exactly why Lilian said *"tengo que puntualizarlo con el."*

🟢 **The good news: splitting is nearly money-neutral this year**, so the question can be settled
calmly rather than under time pressure.

| | Effect of splitting |
|---|---|
| **Self-employment tax** | 🟢 **None.** *2025 Instructions for Schedule SE, page 2:* *"If you had two or more businesses subject to SE tax, your net earnings … are the **combined** net earnings from all of your businesses… **Figure the combined SE tax on one Schedule SE**."* |
| **QBI / Form 8995** | 🟢 **None.** Line 1 has a row per business, but the **(2,005) carryforward is a single line 3 figure sitting BELOW the line 2 total, so by the form's own structure it applies to the AGGREGATE — there is no line 1 row to put it in.** ⓘ *An earlier draft said the instructions "forbid" apportioning it; they say no such thing — the layout settles it.* ⚠️ **But re-check line 3 after adding a second Schedule C: a manually-keyed carryforward is exactly what gets dropped when an entity is added.** |
| **SSTB** | 🟢 **None for 2025** — taxable income 25,197 against the 197,300 threshold. 🔑 **But credit repair plausibly IS an SSTB**, so separating it now, in the year it costs nothing, is what makes a future year defensible. |
| 🔴 **§162(l)** | **This is where it bites.** *Form 7206 (2025), Note:* *"Use a **separate** Form 7206 for each trade or business under which an insurance plan is established"*, and line 4 caps the deduction at **that business's** earned income. **One Marketplace plan can sit under only ONE business.** ⌨️ **Which business the plan is "established under" is a question of FACT, not a firm election** *(Form 7206's own words: "under which an insurance plan **is** established")* — **decide it on the facts, take the larger business where they allow it, and record the reasoning here.** |
| **Form 7206 becomes mandatory** | Two SE income sources means the Form 1040 worksheet is no longer available *(2025 Instructions for Form 7206, page 1)*. |
| **Home office** | Two Schedule Cs means **two** computations, *(2025 Instructions for Schedule C, **page 13**: "If you used your home for more than one business, you will need to file a separate Schedule C for each business")*, and the simplified method's **300 sq ft is shared across all businesses in the home**, not counted twice *(**page 10**: "You are limited to a maximum of 300 square feet for **all of the businesses** you conduct in your home")*. |

⚠️ **And one prior question the file re-opens:** two **`INCORPORATING SERVICES`** charges *(320)*, which
the client calls *"company tax expense"*. **If an entity exists with its own EIN**, some of this income
may not belong on a Schedule C in its present form at all, and organisation costs are **not** a current
expense by default. ⛔ **Unsettled — it is question 7 in §13H.**
