### 13F · ⚖️ THE CLIFF — and the correction that matters most

🛑 **§11A settled the §162(l) deduction at 1,625 at the income AS FILED. That figure is now doing far
more work than it was asked to do**, and the way it does it is not the naive one.

🔑 **THE METHOD POINT — and it is narrower than an earlier draft of this section claimed.** Form 8962's
**Table 5** is NOT the route by which a self-employed taxpayer with APTC finds the repayment limitation.
**Pub. 974 (2025), Instructions for Worksheet X, Line 25 — verbatim:**
> *"Also, enter this amount on **line 28 of the Form 8962 you attach to your tax return** if you are
> required to complete that line **and you do not complete Worksheet Y**. **Do not enter an amount from
> Table 5 in the Form 8962 instructions.**"*

ⓘ *Worksheet Y covers only the four deductions/exclusions Pub. 974 lists, none of which he has — so the
condition is satisfied, but it IS a condition and an earlier draft's ellipsis hid it.*

**Worksheet X Part III is a CASCADE, and the cascade — not an iteration — is what resolves the
circularity.** At each band it subtracts **that band's own cap** from household income and re-tests
the percentage. ⛔ **The band is therefore NOT read off the final AGI.**

✅ **BUT ON THESE FIGURES THE TWO ROUTES AGREE, and §11A already said so** — *"At 378% both give 1,625,
so ATX's automatic Table 5 lookup lands correctly — ⚠️ but had the Worksheet X percentages fallen below
300, the two would diverge."* **They still agree with Tier 1 added**, because Form 8962 line 5 is computed
on household income **as reported** *(61,700 − 1,625 = 60,075 → 398%)*, and Table 5's 300–400% row is also
1,625. ⛔ **So nothing here turns on which table is used, and an earlier draft of this section wrongly
said the choice was worth ~3,450.** 🔑 **What moves the money is whether Schedule 1 line 17 is claimed AT
ALL** — the cascade is only ever reached by a taxpayer taking the deduction. ⚠️ **Knowing the route still
matters for the next client**, whose percentages may straddle a band edge.

🧮 **Run at the income AS FILED, it reproduces §11A exactly — which is what validates the model:**

| Worksheet X | | Value |
|---|---|---|
| 14 | household income *(AGI with SEHI = 0)* | 58,677 |
| 22 | smaller of W-19 or 1,625 *(single)* | 1,625 |
| 23 | line 14 − line 22 | 57,052 |
| 24 | line 23 ÷ 15,060, truncated | **378 < 400** |
| **25** | *"If the result is less than 400, enter $1,625"* | ✅ **1,625 — as §11A found** |

🧮 **Now run it with TIER 1's 3,253.20 added and no expenses:**

| Worksheet X | | Value |
|---|---|---|
| 14 | 58,677 + 3,253.20 − 230 *(the extra half-SE)* | **61,700** |
| 23 | 61,700 − 1,625 | **60,075** |
| 24 | 60,075 ÷ 15,060 = 3.9890 → truncate | 🟢 **398 — still under 400** |
| **25** | | 🟢 **1,625 — THE CAP SURVIVES** |

> 🟢 **SO TIER 1 ALONE DOES NOT CROSS THE CLIFF — *because* the 1,625 is on the return.**
> 🔴 **Leave Schedule 1 line 17 blank and it does**: 61,700 ÷ 15,060 = **409**, the limitation becomes
> **Worksheet W line 2 — the whole APTC, 5,865.60** *(X-24's own words: "If the result is 400 or more,
> enter the amount from Worksheet W, line 2")*, and the repayment jumps from 1,625 to about **5,245**.
> 🧮 *At 400% or more the applicable figure is **0.0850**: 61,700 × 0.0850 = 5,244.50 contribution; allowed
> PTC = 5,865.48 − 5,244.50 = 620.98; line 27 = 5,865.60 − 620.98 = **5,244.62**, uncapped.*
> 🔑 **The §162(l) entry is not housekeeping. At the corrected income it is worth roughly 3,620.**
> 🛑 **AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID 5,076, AND IT WAS WRONG.** 5,076 is not a repayment at all — it is the
> **contribution amount in the 398% scenario** *(60,075 × 0.0845)*, carried across from the wrong column.
> ⚠️ **It also under-reported a repayment at HIGHER income than §9E's own 5,121 at 60,240 — arithmetically
> impossible, and exactly the 0.0823-vs-0.0850 slip §9E was written to prevent.** 🔑 **Crossing the line
> moves the applicable figure to 0.0850; it does not merely remove the cap.**

⚠️ **AND THE MARGIN IS 177 OF ADDITIONAL NET PROFIT** *(165 measured in household income: 61,865 − 61,700)*.
The cascade tips at **line 23 ≥ 60,240**, i.e. at household income
61,865, i.e. at about **3,430 of additional net Schedule C profit**. Tier 1 is 3,253.20.
🛑 **So ANY Tier-2 dollar crosses the cliff unless allowable expenses come with it.**

🔴 **THE RULE THAT FOLLOWS, AND IT IS THE PRACTICAL ONE: THE INCOME AND THE EXPENSES GO ON TOGETHER,
IN ONE PASS.** A return keyed with the new income and not yet the expenses shows a balance due that is
wrong by thousands. ⛔ **It is never quoted to the client, never filed and never printed in that state.**

⚖️ **AND THE CLIFF CUTS BOTH WAYS — this is the reason to take the boring deductions and refuse the
large estimated ones.** A deduction disallowed on a later exam does not merely cost its own tax: it
pushes household income back over 60,240 and **detonates the repayment cap a second time.**
🛑 **So a weak 3,000 deduction is not worth 3,000 of risk — it is worth roughly 4,300.** ⓘ *Said of the
files this session has worked, not of the firm's whole book.*

### 13G · What the expense side can and cannot support

**① 🔴 THE HOUSING RAILS CANNOT BE ADDED UP.** Rent reached the landlord by **five different routes**,
and the workbook split them across two categories:

| Rail | Rows | Amount | Where the tool put it |
|---|---|---|---|
| ERenterPlan / BILT / RP Resident | 17 | 6,353.38 | *Rent / possible home office* |
| AVA Bonterra *(card + billpay)* | 5 | 7,989.77 | *Other expense – needs review* |
| Rivr Lofts RD WEB PMTS | 2 | 3,561.09 | *needs review* |
| **Lvble Repayment** | 19 | **7,273.18** | *needs review* |
| Affirm | 21 | 1,935.23 | *needs review* |
| | | **27,112.65** | |

🔑 **Livble and Affirm FINANCE rent — the deductible event is when the financier pays the landlord, not
when he repays the financier.** So a repayment rail and a landlord rail can be **the same rent twice**.
⚠️ **27,112.65 is close to one plausible year at his own figures** *(AVA ~2,700/mo Jan–May, then RIVR)*,
which suggests the overlap is limited — ⛔ **but "close to plausible" is not a reconciliation.**
**The only thing that settles it is a month-by-month rent schedule: for each of the 12 months, the
rent, the landlord, and which rail paid it.**

**② THE HOME OFFICE IS BOUNDED, AND THE CLIENT HAS ALREADY BOUNDED IT.** His own sheet says **RIVR
Lofts Jun–Dec is `0% automatic`** — *"Studio; work area mixed personal use; coworking included in
rent."* So only **AVA Bonterra, Jan–May, at a claimed 30%** is even in play, and **30% asserted after
the fact is not the exclusive-use finding the rule requires.** The two ceilings:

- **Simplified method** — the rate is **$5 per square foot**. ✅ *Pub. 587 (2025) page 11, "Part-year use
  **or area changes (for simplified method only)**":* *"your deduction is limited to the **average monthly
  allowable square footage** … adding the amount of allowable square feet you used in each month and
  dividing the sum by **12**"*, max 300/month, and **a month with under 15 days of qualified use counts
  as ZERO.** Five months at the 300 cap → (300 × 5) ÷ 12 = **125 sq ft × $5 = 625**.
  ⚠️ 🆕 **THE FIVE MONTHS ARE NOT ESTABLISHED** — his stated dates and his own direct landlord
  payments disagree *(§15D, §6 item ㉘)*. ⛔ **Do not key 625; the month count is the open input.**
- **Actual expenses (Form 8829)** — 30% of the AVA Bonterra rent **and** utilities for those months,
  subject to the **gross-income limit**, which can defer part of it out of 2025.

🟢 **AND THERE IS A KNOCK-ON — but it is NARROWER than it looks, and an earlier draft overstated it.**
A home office that is the principal place of business **of the Schedule C activity** makes home→client
trips **for that activity** deductible business miles rather than commuting *(Rev. Rul. 99-7, which turns
on trips "in the **same trade or business**")*.
⛔ **IT DOES NOTHING FOR THE FOUR W-2 EMPLOYERS.** Those trips are a **different trade or business**, so
they stay commuting — and even if they did not, unreimbursed employee expenses are **suspended through
2025** under §67(g). 🔑 **So the claimed 80% has to be split by WHICH trade or business each mile served
before any of it is a Schedule C expense**, and the W-2 share is worth nothing on this return either way.

**③ THE VEHICLE — §3E's open question, now with the 2025 answer.** 🛑 **The `Vehicle – loan/lease
payment 6,639.72` pool is not deductible AS A LOAN PAYMENT.** *Pub. 463 (2025), page 21,
"Actual Car Expenses"* lists *"Depreciation · **Lease payments** · Registration fees · Licenses · Insurance ·
Repairs · Gas · Garage rent · Tires · Oil · Tolls · Parking fees"* — **no loan payment**, because
principal is the repayment of capital.
⚠️ **BUT READ THE SECOND ITEM IN THAT LIST, AND THE POOL'S OWN LABEL: `loan/LEASE`.** ✅ **The client's
own sheet says `financed $553/mo`**, which on his account makes it principal + interest — **but §3E has
not closed owned-vs-leased, and §13J question 11 still asks it.** 🔑 **If any of it is LEASE payments,
those ARE actual car expenses** *(× the business-use share, less the §280F lease inclusion amount)*, and
only a standard-mileage election would displace them. ⛔ **Do not delete this pool until that is answered.**

🆕 **BUT THE INTEREST INSIDE IT IS DEDUCTIBLE, AND 2025 GIVES IT TWO HOMES.** ✅ **Read off Pub. 463
(2025) page 21, verbatim, 2026-09-18:**
> *"**Interest.** You may be able to take a deduction for interest paid on a car loan **even if you use
> the standard mileage rate**."*
> *"You may be able to deduct **qualified passenger vehicle loan interest** … See Schedule 1-A (Form
> 1040) to see whether you can deduct this interest."*
> *"If you are self-employed and eligible to deduct interest as either qualified passenger vehicle loan
> interest or as business interest, **you can choose to report the deduction on either Schedule 1-A
> (Form 1040) or Schedule C (Form 1040), but you cannot deduct the same amount more than once.**"*

🔑 **AND THE CHOICE IS NOT NEUTRAL HERE — §13F is why.** Schedule C is **above** the AGI line and moves
household income; **Schedule 1-A Part IV is BELOW it** *(1040 line 13b)* and cannot touch the 400% test
at all. ⛔ **With a 177 margin, that makes Schedule C the only version of this deduction that helps** —
🔗 **and §11C's gate still stands: Schedule 1-A Part IV also requires a loan originated after 31 Dec
2024 on a vehicle bought for PERSONAL use, which contradicts the 80% business claim outright.**
⌨️ **One line from the lender's 2025 year-end statement gives the interest** — ⚠️ **but the Schedule C
route then needs it multiplied by the business-use share** *(Pub. 463 p.21: "if you use your car 60% for
business, you can deduct 60% of the interest on Schedule C")*, **which ④ below says cannot be keyed yet.**

**④ 🔴 NO MILEAGE LOG — but "no log, no deduction" is TOO STRONG, and the correct answer is a request,
not a refusal.** *Pub. 463 (2025) page 35:* *"**Note: You can't deduct amounts that you approximate or
estimate**"*, and the percentage is fixed as **business miles ÷ total miles**. So **the 80% cannot be
keyed.** ✅ **But page 36 gives the cure, verbatim:**
> *"**What if I Have Incomplete Records?** If you don't have complete records to prove an element of an
> expense, then you must prove the element with: • **Your own written or oral statement** containing
> specific information about the element, **and** • **Other supporting evidence** that is sufficient to
> establish the element."*
> *"**Sampling.** You can keep an adequate record for **parts** of a tax year and use that record to
> prove the amount of business or investment use for the **entire** year. You must demonstrate by other
> evidence that the periods for which an adequate record is kept are **representative of the use
> throughout the tax year**."*

🔑 **And the business-PURPOSE element may be proved circumstantially** — Pub. 463's own example:
*"the nature of your work, such as making deliveries, provides circumstantial evidence of the use of
your car for business purposes."* ⚠️ **The cost, time, place and date elements still need direct or
documentary evidence** — a calendar, job schedules, invoices. **That is what to ask him for.**

**⑤ AND THE METHOD ELECTION IS A GATE NOBODY HAS OPENED.** *Pub. 463 (2025) page 20:* *"If you want to
use the standard mileage rate for a car **you own**, you must choose to use it **in the first year the
car is available for use in your business**"*, and *"If you use the standard mileage rate for a year,
you can't deduct … depreciation, lease payments, maintenance and repairs, gasoline …, oil, insurance, or
vehicle registration fees."* 🔗 **§3E is already chasing the date placed in service — this is the same
question and it decides everything else**: under standard mileage (**70¢ for 2025**) the fuel 3,290.79,
insurance 3,216.51 and the whole loan pool are **inside the rate** and only tolls/parking 474.58 and the
interest ride on top. ⚠️ **For a LEASED car the rate must be used for the entire lease period.**
ⓘ **A §179 or depreciation claim is a third route and is NOT recommended blind**: it needs **more than
50% business use** *(the same missing log)*, and *Pub. 463 page 22:* *"A car first used for personal
purposes can't qualify for the deduction in a later year when its use changes to business."*

**⑥ MEALS — 50%, and 2,959.36 is unsupportable as it stands.** There is no 100% restaurant rule in 2025.
*"~75% business-related, meetings with realtors"* across **81 rows** is a characterisation, not a record:
the elements are **amount, date, place, business purpose and the business relationship of the person
fed — per meal.** 🔑 **Best case on his own story is 2,959.36 × 75% × 50% = 1,109.76**, so **81 rows of
reconstruction buys about 1,100** — ⛔ **and Julia's own Q2 2026 ruling excludes entertainment and
office meals whatever the purpose was.**

**⑦ CREDIT-CARD INTEREST 4,338.00 — defensible today: ZERO.** Interest follows the **use of the loan
proceeds**, traced charge by charge; there is no percentage to apply to a total. Personal credit-card
interest is **never** deductible and has no itemised home either. ⛔ **On a card whose identified
business charges are a few hundred dollars, the tracing will not repay the hours.**

**⑧ FINES ARE NEVER DEDUCTIBLE, and the defence fee follows the offence.** The **Ticket Clinic** charges
and any traffic fine come out in full — the client's own sheet already says so. ⚠️ **Small in dollars,
loud in signal.**

**⑨ WHAT ACTUALLY SURVIVES TRIAGE**, before any business-percentage haircut:

| | |
|---|---|
| Software / business services | 261.03 |
| Licences / legal / filing | 229.05 |
| Bank / card fees *(business account only, penalties excluded)* | 431.00 |
| Supplies / equipment ⚠️ *receipts required; anything lasting beyond a year is capital, not a supply* | 2,340.21 |
| Phone / internet ⚠️ *at a substantiated share, not the asserted 85%* | 1,128.89 |
| Travel | 113.50 |
| | **4,503.68** |

🛑 **AND 3,430 IS NOT AN EXPENSE TARGET — an earlier draft of this line misread it.** §13F defines it as
the **additional net PROFIT** at which the cascade tips.
🟢 **On Tier 1 alone (3,253.20) NO expense is needed** — the headroom is 177 of profit.
🔴 **But if Tier 2's 8,048.05 also lands, staying under needs ≈ 7,870 of ALLOWABLE expenses**
*(3,253.20 + 8,048.05 − 3,430 = 7,871)*, **and this 4,503.68 list does not reach it even if every line
survives.** 🔑 **It is still the safest 4,503.68 on the file, and a far better place to stand than the
vehicle and meal pools** — it is simply **not sufficient on its own**, which is the whole reason §13E's
tiers have to be settled before anything is keyed.

### 13H · 🔴 UNFILED FORMS 1099-NEC — and Schedule C line I currently says "No"

The client's own sheet confirms he paid **contractors, referral commissions, lead generation and a
paralegal** — naming **Andriy Melnyk, Dillon Mathewson, Iana Sytnychenko, Sabrina Morales-Lyles,
Pavel Striliuk, Viacheslav Honcharenko, Federico Landaburu, NEW MOON I INC** and **CLARITY CREDIT LLC**.

⛔ **TWO EXCLUSIONS COME FIRST, BEFORE ANYONE COUNTS A DOLLAR — and neither was in the first draft:**
① a payee that is a **CORPORATION** is generally outside Form 1099-NEC altogether *(Reg. §1.6041-3(p); the
exceptions are attorneys and medical)* — **`NEW MOON I INC` on its face, and any LLC taxed as one, which
only a W-9 settles**; and ② an amount paid **BY CREDIT CARD** is the processor's to report on a Form
1099-K, **not his on a 1099-NEC** *(Reg. §1.6041-1(a)(1)(iv))* — and the workbook covers an Apple Card and
a Chase Prime Visa. 🔑 **So only the Zelle and ACH outflows to NON-CORPORATE payees are even in scope.**

🔑 **Then the test is the ANNUAL TOTAL PER PAYEE, not per payment** — so those outflows must be
aggregated by name before anyone concludes they are under the line. ⓘ *Two payees are over on a single
transfer alone (1,350 and 1,000), which settles their annual totals without any aggregation; it does not
make the per-payment amount the test.* ⚠️ **The threshold for 2025 is 600; the increase to 2,000
applies only to payments made after 31 December 2025 and does not help him.** ⛔ **Zelle does not
rescue him** — the Form 1099-K carve-out covers payment cards and third-party settlement
organizations, and a bank-to-bank Zelle transfer is generally neither.

⏳ **Filing was due 2 February 2026 — over seven months ago.** 🟢 **One myth to kill: a missing
1099-NEC does NOT disallow the deduction.** The exposure is penalties, and it is separate from the
expense.

🛑 **AND IT LANDS ON THE RETURN ITSELF.** Schedule C **line I** asks whether he made payments requiring
a Form 1099, and **line J** asks whether he did or will file them. 🔴 **Line I currently reads "No".**
If that is wrong it is a false answer **under penalties of perjury**, on the very line the IRS uses to
select these cases. ⌨️ **Both are TYPED. Neither can be left as it is without an answer.**
🔗 **`Viacheslav Honcharenko` — one of the PAYEES — is a JK client; and so is `VOXAGO LLC`, which is a
PAYER, not a payee** *(§13E Tier 1 — an inflow of 266.20 TO him)*. ⛔ **An earlier draft called Voxago a
payee, inside this same paragraph about a possibly-false answer under penalties of perjury.** Both are the
same pattern as the VoiceCapital discovery in §4 decision 6 and are a **conflict to name, not to use.**

### 13I · ⛔ WHAT THIS PASS DID **NOT** DO

1. **Nothing was keyed.** No figure in §13 has been entered anywhere. The return is unchanged.
2. **No expense was accepted.** Every number in §13G is a pool, not a deduction.
3. **The §162(l) figure was NOT re-derived at Tier-2 income.** §13F's cascade was run at Tier 1 only.
   🔴 **Re-run Worksheet X Part III at whatever income is finally settled** — the band can move, and it
   is the band, not the AGI, that sets line 28.
4. **The December Chase window was not obtained**, so no completeness statement is available.
5. **Nothing was written to Double**, and no figure from this section is in the client-intelligence
   file or in `FOLLOW-UPS.md`.
6. 🔴 ⚠️ **HOW THE WORKBOOK WAS READ, AND IT WAS NOT THE REDACTOR.** Lilian uploaded the `.xlsx` into the
   session directly; it was parsed **locally, with a stdlib script**, and never went through
   [`tools/redact-doc/`](../../../tools/redact-doc/) — which is **PDF-only** and could not have taken it.
   🔑 **Recorded because §3B and §4 decision 6 set the precedent that an unmasked channel is NAMED, not
   assumed** — this is the **third** such channel on this client. ⛔ **Nothing from the identity block was
   written anywhere**, and the workbook carries no SSN, no full account number and no login; ⚠️ **but the
   masking a redactor read would have applied was not applied**, so the whole file was visible to the
   session — including the account nicknames and the full payee names.

   Did either send you a **1099** form? If you never got one, just tell us — we still have to include it.

   you. **Did you set up a company?** If yes: its exact name, the state, does it have **its own bank
   account**, and was any of the money above paid **to the company** rather than to you personally?

**D · THE ONE WORTH THE MOST MONEY** *(settles §11A's open gate — §13F)*

   of the places you worked for — **the restaurant, catering, events or concessions employers** — offer
   you health insurance that **they** paid part of? 🔑 **We do not need to know whether you signed up.
   We need to know whether you were ALLOWED to, and for which months.** If you are not sure, please ask

    one line. 🔑 **The monthly payment itself is not deductible; the interest inside it is.**
13. We cannot use a percentage on its own — the IRS does not accept an estimate. But you **do not need
    a full log**: send us anything showing **where you drove and when** — your **calendar**, the job
    schedules from your employers, or your bookings. 🔑 **Even a few well-documented months can carry
    the whole year if they are typical.**

    it, ate in it, or used it for anything else, tell us — it changes the answer.

    because paying rent through a financing company shows up twice in your statements.**

    **full name** and roughly **how much in total for the whole year**. ⚠️ **We need the year total
    per person, not each payment.** 🔑 **If anyone reached $600 for the year, a form was due and we

**H · THE GAP WE CANNOT CLOSE OURSELVES** *(settles §13E Tier 3)*

    covering **2–31 December**. 🔑 **December is your busiest month and we cannot finish without it.**
19. Do you have **any other bank or card account** used in 2025 that is not in this file?

**I · RECEIPTS WORTH CHASING, BECAUSE THEY ARE THE SAFE ONES** *(settles §13G⑨)*

> the record of what the law says, but it is not the question list any more.

### 14B · 🗂️ The Maxratings 1099 — where it is, and why nobody found it

### 14C · 🚗 2024 DEDUCTED FUEL WITH NO PERCENTAGE — and IF it was car fuel, that governs 2025's method

✅ **Answering Lilian's question: yes, 2024 deducted fuel.** The 2024 Schedule C Part V carried
**`GAS & FUEL` 2,080** *(plus Phone 870; Part V total 2,950)* — 🔗 **§3E has the full working.**
⚠️ **Whether it was CAR fuel is NOT established** *(§3E ⑤)* — and an earlier draft of this section
headed itself *"2024 DID DEDUCT THE CAR"*, which its own body then contradicted two paragraphs later.

**Three things about how it was done, because all three bear on 2025:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Where** | In **Part V / `Other expenses`** — ⛔ **line 9 was left blank**, and Schedule C **Part IV was entirely empty** |
| **At what percentage** | 🔴 **None. The whole amount was deducted**, which asserts 100% business use |
| **Which method that IS** | 🔴 **Deducting actual fuel IS the actual-expense method**, wherever on the form it was written |

🛑 **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR 2025.** *2025 Instructions for Schedule C, Line 9 — verbatim, BOTH bullets,
because an earlier draft quoted only the first and made the rule look narrower than it is:*
> *"You can take the standard mileage rate for 2025 **only if** you: • **Owned** the vehicle and used
> the standard mileage rate for the first year you placed the vehicle in service, **or** • **Leased**
> the vehicle and are using the standard mileage rate **for the entire lease period**."*

⚠️ **THREE THINGS ARE NOT ESTABLISHED and they decide it — §6 items ⑬ and ⑭, and §13J question E11:**
1. **Whether the 2024 `GAS & FUEL` was CAR fuel or EQUIPMENT fuel.** Part V says only `GAS & FUEL`,
   and on a catering activity either is ordinary. 🔑 **Answer this FIRST** — *(§3E ⑤)* **if it was
   equipment fuel there was no actual-expense claim at all and the 2025 election is simply open.**
2. **When the vehicle was placed in service for business.**
3. 🔴 **Whether the car is OWNED or LEASED — and an earlier draft of this section dropped this one
   entirely.**

⚖️ **AND THE ANSWER IS NOT THE SAME FOR THE TWO, WHICH IS §3E ④'s CENTRAL HOLDING — carried here
intact rather than flattened into "may close":**
- **LEASED** → the two rules agree: the rate must be used *"for the entire lease period"*, and
  claiming actual car expenses on a leased car is on Pub. 463's *"not allowed"* list explicitly.
  **A 2024 actual-expense year rules it out for that lease.**
- **OWNED** → ⚖️ **they point different ways.** Rule A *(the election must be made in the first year
  the car is available for business)* bites; but **every owned-car entry on the "not allowed" list is
  DEPRECIATION-based, and 2024's line 13 was blank.** 🔑 **§3E records this as a genuine argument a
  reviewer will raise**, not as a loophole.
- ⛔ **And the logically prior question is also unsettled: whether the 2024 return made an ELECTION AT
  ALL** — it claimed nothing on line 9, gave no vehicle information, and identified nothing as a vehicle.

🛑 **§3E does not conclude the rate is closed, and neither does this section. The position is Lilian's
and Julia's to take.**

ⓘ *A Form 4562 may sit on page 12 of the 2024 PDF; it was never extracted (§3E).*

### 14D · ⌨️ MILEAGE IN ATX — what the client has to supply beyond the miles

🔑 **Lilian's question: besides odometer start/end and business miles, what else?** Read off the
**2025 Schedule C, Part IV**, verbatim — these are **fields on the form**, not evidence requests:

| Line | The form asks | What to get from him |
|---|---|---|
| **43** | *"When did you place your vehicle in service for business purposes? (month/day/year)"* | 🔴 **A DATE** — and see §14C, it also decides whether the rate is available at all |
| **44a** | Business miles | the business figure |
| 🔴 **44b** | **Commuting miles** | ⚠️ **A SEPARATE NUMBER he has not been asked for** — with four W-2 employers this is not zero, and it is not the same as "personal" |
| **44c** | Other (personal) miles | the remainder |
| **45** | *"Was your vehicle available for personal use during off-duty hours?"* | Yes / No |
| **46** | *"Do you (or your spouse) have another vehicle available for personal use?"* | Yes / No — 🔑 **his own sheet already answers it: *"only vehicle in 2025"*** |
| **47a / 47b** | *"Do you have evidence to support your deduction?"* / *"If 'Yes,' is the evidence written?"* | Yes / No, twice |

⚠️ **44a + 44b + 44c must total the year's miles**, which is what makes the odometer readings useful —
they are the control total, not an input on their own.

🟢 **AND THREE THINGS RIDE *ON TOP OF* THE MILEAGE RATE — not inside it** *(2025 rate: **70¢**)*:

1. **Business parking and tolls** — *Pub. 463 p.21.* ✅ **Already in his file** *(the SunPass and
   parking lines)*.
2. 🔑 **The business share of the CAR LOAN INTEREST** — *Pub. 463 (2025) p.21, verbatim:* *"You may be
   able to take a deduction for interest paid on a car loan **even if you use the standard mileage
   rate**."* ⌨️ **One line off the lender's 2025 year-end statement** — this is a document to request.
   ⚠️ 🆕 **ONLY IF THE CAR IS FINANCED.** On a **LEASE there is no loan interest to claim**, so this
   line does not exist and there is no such statement to get. 🔑 **§15C establishes the counterparty
   (a Capital One auto account) and NOT the contract** — twelve identical monthly payments fit a
   lease as well as a loan — **so §14H item 3's owned-or-leased question comes first.**
   ⚠️ **And 2025 gives it two homes — Schedule C or Schedule 1-A, never both.** 🔗 **§13G③ explains
   why Schedule C is the one that helps here.**
3. **The business share of state/local personal property tax on the vehicle**, if any.

⛔ **And what the rate REPLACES, so it is not asked for twice** — *Pub. 463 (2025) p.20, verbatim:*
*"You can't deduct **depreciation, lease payments**, maintenance and repairs, gasoline (including
gasoline taxes), **oil**, insurance, or vehicle registration fees."* ⚠️ **Lease payments and oil were
missing from an earlier draft of this list, and the lease one matters here** — §13G③ holds a
`Vehicle – loan/lease payment` pool and the owned-vs-leased question is still open.

⌨️ **AND THE ENTRY ITSELF, from the same Line 9 instruction:** *"Multiply the number of business miles
driven by **0.70** … **Add to this amount your parking fees and tolls**; and **Enter the total on line
9**."* 🔑 **One figure on line 9 — the mileage and the tolls together, not two lines.**

🛑 **PART IV IS NOT ALWAYS THE RIGHT PLACE, AND THE INSTRUCTIONS ADD A CONDITION THE FORM'S CAPTION
DOES NOT.** *2025 Instructions for Schedule C, "Information on your vehicle", verbatim:*
> *"**1.** Complete Schedule C, **Part IV**, if **(a)** you are claiming the standard mileage rate,
> you lease your vehicle, or your vehicle is fully depreciated; **and (b) you are not required to
> file Form 4562 for any other reason.** … **2.** Complete **Form 4562, Part V**, if you are claiming
> depreciation on your vehicle **or you are required to file Form 4562 for any other reason**."*

⚠️ **So "no vehicle depreciation" is not enough on its own — condition (b) is about the WHOLE return.**
🔗 **§13G⑨ flags 2,340.21 of supplies/equipment where anything lasting beyond a year is capital**, so
a Form 4562 may be required anyway, and the vehicle information would move with it.
🔑 **Decide it explicitly rather than defaulting to Part IV** *(§3E ⑥ step 5 already said this)*.

### 14E · 🏠 THE HOME OFFICE — what he gave, and the one input he did NOT give

✅ **What the client stated, in his own words** *(his `Confirmed Rules` and `Accountant Handoff`)*:

| | |
|---|---|
| **AVA Bonterra, Jan–May 2025** | *"Home office allocation · **30%** · 2BR; **one bedroom used exclusively as office**; rent approx. **$2,700/mo**"* |
| **AVA Bonterra utilities, Jan–May** | *"30% · apply **only** to utilities tied to the AVA period"* |
| 🔴 **RIVR Lofts / Livble, Jun–Dec** | *"**0% automatic** · Personal/mixed housing · **Studio; work area mixed personal use; coworking included in rent**"* |

🟢 **He has already answered the two questions that usually stall this**: he says the AVA room was
**used exclusively** as an office, and **he himself excluded the second residence.** So the claim is
**five months at one address**, which is what he is asking us to take.

🔴 **WHAT IS MISSING IS ARITHMETIC, NOT A JUDGEMENT: HE GAVE A PERCENTAGE, AND BOTH METHODS WANT
SQUARE FEET.**

| Method | The form's own inputs |
|---|---|
| **Form 8829** | **line 1** *"Area used regularly and exclusively for business"* · **line 2** *"Total area of home"* → line 3 computes the % |
| **Simplified** *(Schedule C line 30)* | *"Simplified method filers only: Enter the total square footage of **(a) your home**"* — and **(b)** the part used for business |

⌨️ **So the ask is two numbers: the square footage of the office room, and the square footage of the
apartment.** ⛔ **A 30% estimate cannot be typed into either form.**
ⓘ **Also needed:** the **months** the office was used *(Jan–May — five)*, because the simplified
method prorates on average monthly square footage, and the **actual rent and utilities paid in those
months** *(his `$2,700/mo` is his own approximation)*.
⛔ **And the transactions CANNOT simply be summed to get it** — §13G① shows the rent reached the
landlord by **five rails**, two of which *finance* the same rent, so a repayment rail and a landlord
rail can be the same money twice. 🔑 **The month-by-month rent schedule is the only thing that settles
it, and it is a CLIENT ASK, not a firm derivation** *(§14H item 2b)*.

is reportable either way. **It changes only whether an AUR notice is coming.**

### 14G · ✂️ SPLITTING THE SCHEDULE C IN TWO — what it costs and one caution on the labels

🔗 **§13D has the full analysis. The short form: the split is nearly money-neutral.** One Schedule SE
combines both; the QBI carryforward applies to the aggregate; SSTB does not bite at his income. **The
only place it bites is §162(l)** — one health plan sits under **one** business, capped at that
business's earned income — and the **simplified method's 300 sq ft CAP is shared across both
businesses**, never counted twice *(it is the cap, not his area — §14E is the reason his area is still
unknown)*.

🟡 **THE CAUTION BELOW IS NARROWED, NOT SPENT.** ✅ **Julia answered its FIRST half on 2026-09-20
(§15A): there ARE two activities, and the catering one is real** — so *"is this one business or
two?"* is settled as a question about his **work** *(§13J **A2**)*. ⛔ **Do not re-raise THAT.**
🛑 **TWO ACTIVITIES IS NOT YET TWO SCHEDULE Cs, AND NOTHING HERE LICENSES KEYING A SECOND ONE.** A Schedule C
is filed for an activity that **traded in 2025**, and none of the 2025 1099 payers is a caterer, so
**whether catering traded at all is unestablished — §15A.** 🔑 **§13D's two-Schedule-C analysis is
what to APPLY once the templates come back**, not the shape to key from now. ⛔ **A catering template
returning costs and no income means ONE Schedule C.**
🆕 ✅ **2026-09-22 — THE TEMPLATE CAME BACK WITH EXACTLY THAT, AND THIS RULE STANDS UNCHANGED**
*(§16D)*. 🛑 **BUT THE PHRASE "ONE SCHEDULE C" IS NOW AMBIGUOUS AND §16G TURNS ON THE DIFFERENCE:**
**here it means CREDIT REPAIR ALONE, with catering off the return** — ⛔ **it does NOT mean adding the
two activities' figures together onto a single form**, which is a third shape nobody has chosen and
which costs about **1,464** of §162(l) deduction. 🔗 **§16G names all three.**
🔴 **Its SECOND half is still live, and the 2026-09-20 email did the exact thing it warned against:
it named the two activities to the client** — *«кейтеринг»* and *«ремонт кредита»* — **instead of
asking him to split by WHICH WORK each expense belongs to.** 🔑 **That was a deliberate trade**, and
the reason is §15A: Julia named the activities, so asking him *"what are your businesses?"* a second
time would have read as the firm not having listened. ⚠️ **But the risk the caution identified did
not go away with it** — he may still file W-2 commuting and W-2 meals under *«кейтеринг»*, because on
this return the catering work IS the W-2 employment.
🛡️ **THE COMPENSATING CONTROL IS §13B, AND IT MUST ACTUALLY BE RUN.** §13B established that the four
payroll employers' **net** pay is already sitting in the workbook's "unclassified" pool — Professional
Concessions ties to the penny. **So when the catering template comes back, its income and expense
lines are matched against §13B's payroll traces BEFORE anything is keyed**: a *«кейтеринг»* line that
reconciles to a W-2 employer is W-2 activity, not Schedule C. ⛔ **Without that pass, the caution's
failure mode arrives silently.**
⚠️ **AND JULIA IS NOT THE CLIENT.** What §15A settles is what **the firm** understands his activities
to be; **he** has not yet described either one. 🔗 **§13J question A1 — what the work actually was,
per payer — is STILL OPEN and was NOT asked in this email.**
ⓘ *The original caution, kept because its reasoning is still the reason the templates are split — and
because it is now the checklist for reading the answer:*

⚠️ **ONE CAUTION ON HOW THE REQUEST IS WORDED TO HIM, because it changes the answer he gives.**
Lilian's framing is *"catering y reparación de crédito"*. 🔑 **But on this return the CATERING work is
the W-2 employment** — Creative Edge, Professional Concessions, DO & CO and Compass Group are all
payroll *(§3A)*, and **§13B shows their net pay is already sitting in the workbook's "unclassified"
pool.** ⛔ **None of the three 1099 payers is a caterer:** Maxratings *(credit repair)*, VoiceCapital
*(telecom)* and Pro Title Agency *(title)*.
🔑 **So the safer ask is "split your business expenses by WHICH WORK they belong to", listing the
payers, rather than naming the two businesses for him** — otherwise he may file his W-2 commuting and
meals under "catering" and we inherit the error. 🔗 **§13J question A1 is still the one that settles
what the second business actually is.**

### 16D · 🔴 THE CATERING SCHEDULE C — the test came back, and the answer is "it did not trade"

#### ① The test was set in advance, and this is its result

🔑 **Both §14G and §15A wrote the test down before the templates went out — in DIFFERENT words, and
the difference matters.**
> **§15A:** *"if the catering template comes back with expenses and no income, the activity did not
> trade in 2025 and the Schedule C says so."*
> **§14G, which goes further and states the consequence:** *"A catering template returning costs and
> no income means **ONE Schedule C**."*

**What came back: catering revenue `0.00`, catering expenses `6,449.92`.**

🛑 **SO THE PREDICTED ANSWER IS THE ACTUAL ANSWER, AND IT HAS TO BE ACTED ON RATHER THAN NOTED.**
⛔ **This is not a small presentational point.** 🔑 **§14G's rule already answers the SHAPE** —
credit repair alone, catering off the return *(§16G shape **C**)*. **What it does NOT answer is the
POSITION underneath it**, which is the three bullets below. ⚠️ **And §16G names a THIRD shape that
nobody has chosen and that a preparer reaches by accident, at a cost of about 1,464.**

**Three things follow, and none of them is settled by the client:**

- 🔴 **A Schedule C with gross receipts of zero and a 6,449.92 loss is a position, not a data entry.**
  The questions behind it are whether the activity **operated at all** in 2025, whether the costs are
  **current** expenses of a going trade or business or **start-up** costs under §195, and whether a
  loss with **no GROSS receipts** invites the profit-motive question ⓘ *(gross receipts — the
  revenue line. ⛔ Not paper receipts: that sense is what the 2026-09-19 posture retired, and the
  word should not drift back in here.)*. ⚖️ **A position is Lilian's to take**
  *(delivery rule 7)*, and it needs a fact from him first: **did he do any catering work in 2025?**
- 🔴 **70% OF THE VEHICLE IS ALLOCATED TO THE ACTIVITY WITH NO REVENUE.** He splits the car
  **70% catering / 30% credit repair**, so the larger share of the car lands on the Schedule C that
  earned nothing. ⛔ **That is not a substantiation question and the posture does not retire it** — it
  decides **which Schedule C** the deduction sits on, which is a typed allocation on two forms.
  ⚠️ **And it interacts with §13G④**, which already holds that the 80% must first be split by **which
  trade or business each mile served**, with the four W-2 employers' miles worth nothing on this
  return either way.
- ✅ **It also resolves §14G's other half in his favour, and that is worth saying:** he **did** split
  the two activities rather than filing W-2 costs under "catering". 🔑 **The compensating control
  §14G required — matching the catering lines against §13B's four payroll employers — is still owed,
  and it cannot run until the attachments are readable** *(§16A)*.
  🆕 🔴 **2026-09-22 (later): THE BLOCKER IS GONE, AND THE CONTROL AS DESIGNED CANNOT BE RUN AT ALL.**
  §17E shows the catering P&L carries **no itemised transactions** — only **seven allocated
  percentages of pooled totals.** ⛔ **There is nothing to match line by line.** 🔑 **The W-2
  commuting-and-meals risk §14G identified has not gone away — it has moved INSIDE the fuel, tolls
  and meals POOLS**, where it is larger and harder to see. 🛠️ **The pool-level version is now the
  control: test §3A's four employers against those pools in the 1,519 rows. §17J carries it.**

#### ② The vehicle METHOD is a decision he has taken for us

⚠️ **He states the actual-expense method as a given.** ⛔ **§3E and §14C do not treat it as settled:**
§14C keeps §3E's holding intact — **three facts decide the 2025 method**, and §3E does **not** conclude
the standard-mileage route is closed. 🔑 **His answer supplies one of the three** *(financed, not
leased — so the "leased car" branch that would close standard mileage outright does not apply)*, and
**leaves the others open**.
🟢 **ONE THING HIS ANSWER DOES SETTLE, and it closes an open fork from 2026-09-20:** the
actual-expense method requires **depreciation**, so **Form 4562 is filed**, so the vehicle information
goes in **Form 4562 Part V**, not Schedule C Part IV. ⛔ **Part IV's own caption makes them mutually
exclusive** — *"…and are not required to file Form 4562 for this business"* — **so §14H item 3's
"which branch" question is answered by his own method choice.**
⚖️ **But whether the firm ACCEPTS that method is Lilian's call, not his**, and it is worth money in
both directions *(§13G③, §14C)*.

### 16E · 🔴 THE REPORTED INCOME IS **BELOW** THE FORMS 1099 THE FIRM HOLDS — and that changes what the return ATTACHES

🛑 **This is the most serious single item in the reply, and it is arithmetic, not judgement.**

| | Amount |
|---|---|
| Schedule C line 1 **as filed** *(the three 1099s — §3B)* | **19,530.00** |
| **+ Tier 1**, established from his own workbook *(§13E)* | **3,253.20** |
| **= what this paper had established as reportable** | **22,783.20** |
| 🔴 **What he now proposes to report** *(19,246.20 credit repair + 0.00 catering)* | **19,246.20** |
| 🔴 **Short of the three 1099s alone** | **283.80** |
| 🔴 **Short of the established figure** | **3,537.00** |

📖 **2025 Instructions for Schedule C, "Line 1", read off `i1040sc.pdf` at irs.gov on 2026-09-22,
verbatim:**
> *"If you received one or more Forms 1099-NEC, be sure line 1 includes amounts properly shown on
> your Forms 1099-NEC. **If the total amounts that were reported in box 1 of Forms 1099-NEC are more
> than the total you are reporting on line 1, attach a statement explaining the difference.**"*

🔴 **SO A STATEMENT IS NOW REQUIRED, AND §13E SAYS THE OPPOSITE.** §13E reads:
*"⛔ Do not attach the Schedule C line 1 reconciling statement: that is required only when the 1099
total EXCEEDS line 1, **which will not be the case**."* ⚠️ **That sentence was correct when it was
written** — at 19,530.00 reported against 19,530.00 of 1099s — **and the client's own figures have
made it false.** 🔑 **It is struck in place at §13E rather than deleted, so the reversal is visible.**

⚠️ **AND THE SHORTFALL SITS ON ONE FORM, NOT SPREAD BETWEEN TWO — but be exact about why.** ⛔ **It
is NOT that all three payers are credit repair:** §14G classifies them **Maxratings (credit repair),
VoiceCapital (telecom), Pro Title Agency (title)**, and 🔴 **§13J A1 — what the work for each payer
actually was — is STILL OPEN** *(§15A, §6 ㉕)*. ✅ **What settles it is simpler: catering reports zero
revenue**, so whichever activity each payer belongs to, **none of the 19,246.20 lands on the catering
form.**
⚠️ **A required statement MAY be a hard e-file blocker, and that is NOT established for this one.**
📖 *[`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §4B part 6, verbatim:* **"Several
positions require an attached statement, and **some** are hard e-file blockers — the software refuses
to send until one exists."** ⛔ **Its worked example is an NOL carryover on Schedule 1, not a Schedule C
line 1 reconciliation**, and the IRS instruction quoted above says only *"attach a statement"* —
**nothing about transmission.** 🛠️ **HOW TO ESTABLISH IT: try the transmission in ATX with the
statement absent and read what it says.** 🔑 **Either way the statement is REQUIRED by the
instruction** — whether it also stops the e-file only changes how loudly to say so. **And it is
drafted by the firm, from reasoning the return never shows.** ⛔ **It cannot be written until §16F settles what the
2,987.00 actually is**, because that is most of the difference the statement would have to explain.

### 16F · 🔴 THE 2,987.00 "REFUND" — his own two statements point opposite ways, and there is a test

| Who said it | When | What they said |
|---|---|---|
| **The client, on his own expense workbook** | 2026-09-18 *(§14F)* | **`Work income · Business income · Work`** — 🔑 **his own three labels** |
| **The client, by email** | **2026-09-22** | *"a refund of my own funds, not income — excluded from revenue"* |

⛔ **Both are the same person, and they cannot both be right.** 🛑 **This is NOT resolved by the
2026-09-19 posture.** The posture says the firm takes the client's information rather than auditing
it; **it does not tell a session which of two contradictory client statements to take**, and
[`method.md`](../../pre-return-review/method.md) **rule 8** is explicit that where two records
disagree you **show both and ask**. ⚠️ **An earlier draft of this section cited "rule 9", which is a
different rule** *(question the frame when several anomalies move together)*.

🔎 **AND THERE IS A MECHANICAL TEST THAT SETTLES IT — the mirror scan**
*([`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §4A-M).* Its principle:
> **A REFUND OF A COST DEDUCTED IN THE SAME YEAR IS NOT INCOME — IT REDUCES THE COST.**

🔑 **A refund has a matching OUTFLOW. Income does not.** §14F records the inflow side precisely —
**2,347.00 by ACH in two deposits, plus 640.00 by Zelle**. ⛔ **The debit side has never been looked
for.** **The scan is: search the 1,519-row extract for payments TO `Trusted Credit Repair`, match on
amount first, then re-group on the descriptor's own reference.**

**Three outcomes, and each is a different line on the return:**

| What the scan finds | What the 2,987.00 is |
|---|---|
| **Matching outflows in 2025** | ✅ **He is right** — a same-year refund **reduces the cost**, and neither row is income |
| **Matching outflows in an EARLIER year** | 🔴 **Income under the tax benefit rule, §111** — §4A-M's own caveat: *"the same-year part is not decoration"* |
| **No outflows at all** | 🔴 **Income**, and his 2026-09-18 label was the right one |

🛠️ **The scan cannot run until the December and September gaps are closed** *(§6 ㉗ and §16B item 10)*
**and ideally not until the attachments are readable**, since his reconciliation note may already
name the offsetting payments. ⛔ **Until it runs, the 2,987.00 stays in Tier 1 and §6 item ㉔ stands.**
⚠️ **AND THE 1099 QUESTION IS SEPARATE AND STILL UNANSWERED** — §14F: the answer *"does not change
what goes on the return… it changes only whether an AUR notice is coming."*

### 16G · 💊 §162(l) COLLAPSES ON A **COMBINED** SCHEDULE C — and splitting is what saves it

⛔ **CONTINGENT — every client figure below is from the EMAIL BODY; nobody has read the attachments
(§16A).**
🔑 **§14G said the split was "nearly money-neutral… the only place it bites is §162(l)". His figures
have made that exception BITE**, and it now runs **in favour** of the split.
ⓘ *The two expense totals used below — credit repair **12,635.69**, catering **6,449.92** — are from
the email body, alongside the revenue figures in §16A's summary.*

📖 **The mechanism is already in this paper.** §11A records **Pub. 974 Worksheet W line 15** as the
**earned-income limit** — at the income as filed, `19,531 − 1,380 = 18,151` — and **Worksheet X line
30** as *"smaller of 28 or W-15"*. **At 18,151 the limit is far above 1,625, so it never bit.**

🧮 **On his figures it bites hard, because the limit follows the net profit of the business the plan
sits under:**

| Scenario | Net profit supporting the plan | Worksheet W-15 | 🔴 **Schedule 1 line 17** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **As filed** | 19,530.00 | 18,151 | **1,625** ✅ |
| 🔴 **ONE combined Schedule C** | `6,610.51 − 6,449.92` = **160.59** | **≈ 160.59** | 🔴 **≈ 161 — the deduction is all but gone** |
| ✅ **TWO Schedule Cs, plan under credit repair** | **6,610.51** | **≈ 6,610.51** | ✅ **1,625 survives** |
| ✅ **Catering not a 2025 trade at all** | **6,610.51** | **≈ 6,610.51** | ✅ **1,625 survives** |

🧮 *Arithmetic: credit repair `19,246.20 − 12,635.69 = 6,610.51`; catering `0.00 − 6,449.92 =
−6,449.92`; combined `160.59`. Combined net earnings from self-employment `160.59 × 0.9235 = 148.30`,
which is **under the §1402(b)(2) 400 floor, so there is no SE tax and no half-SE reduction** — which
is why W-15 lands on the net profit itself.*

🛑 **THREE SHAPES, AND THE PHRASE "ONE SCHEDULE C" NAMES TWO OF THEM — which is why they are spelled
out here rather than counted:**

| Shape | What it means | §162(l) |
|---|---|---|
| **A · COMBINED** | both activities' figures **added onto a single form** | 🔴 **≈ 161** |
| **B · SPLIT** | two Schedule Cs, plan under credit repair | ✅ **1,625** |
| **C · CREDIT REPAIR ALONE** | catering off the return entirely — 🔑 **this is what §14G means by "ONE Schedule C"** | ✅ **1,625** |

🔑 **§14G's rule points at C.** ⚠️ **C is fine for §162(l) — but §16H shows it is the shape that puts
him at 299% of the poverty line, 134 from the band edge, and it is the ONE shape where the manual
worksheet and ATX disagree.** 🛑 **The expensive outcome for the DEDUCTION is A, which nobody has
chosen and which is simply what happens if a preparer adds his two P&Ls together.** ⛔ **Nothing on
the return flags it** — Schedule 1 line 17 would come out smaller and foot either way.
⚠️ **So this is not an argument against §14G. It is an argument against A.**
⚠️ **This is a REASON TO SPLIT, not an argument that catering is a trade or business.** §16D① is a
separate question and is answered first.

### 16H · ⚖️ THE CLIFF MOVES **DOWN**, AND OUT OF THE BAND §11A VALIDATED

🔑 **§13F worked this cliff in one direction — income going ON.** His expenses move it hard the
other way, and that has its own consequence.

🧮 **Order of magnitude, from the as-filed position in §13F:**

| | |
|---|---|
| Worksheet X line 14 **as filed** | **58,677** |
| Change in Schedule C net | `160.59 − 19,530.00` = **−19,369.41** |
| Add back the half-SE deduction that disappears | **+1,379.83** *(§11A's 1,380)* |
| 🔴 **Household income, approximately** | **≈ 40,690** |

🛑 **AND THE THREE SHAPES DO NOT LAND TOGETHER — AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS SECTION SAID THEY DID, AND
IT WAS WRONG IN THE MOST DAMAGING DIRECTION.** ⛔ It computed household income **once**, off a
Schedule C net of 160.59, and reported *"shapes B and C → ≈ 259%… the finding does not turn on the
choice."* 🔴 **Shape C is not 259%. It is 299%** — because **C takes catering OFF the return**, so the
net is **6,610.51**, net SE earnings are **6,104.81 — above the §1402(b)(2) 400 floor** — and **the SE
tax and the half-SE deduction both come back.**

| Shape | Sch C net | NESE | SE tax | half-SE | X line 14 | X line 22 | X line 23 | **% FPL** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A · combined** | 160.59 | 148.30 | 0 | 0 | 40,687.42 | **160.59** | 40,526.83 | **269** |
| **B · split** | 160.59 | 148.30 | 0 | 0 | 40,687.42 | 1,625 | 39,062.42 | **259** |
| 🔴 **C · credit repair alone** | **6,610.51** | 6,104.81 | **934.04** | **467.02** | **46,670.32** | 1,625 | **45,045.32** | 🔴 **299** |

🧮 *Same three-line model as the table above, run per shape. It reproduces §11A's 1,380 half-SE at the
as-filed net, so the method is this paper's own.*

🔴 **TWO THINGS FOLLOW, AND C IS THE SHAPE §14G'S RULE POINTS AT** *(§16G)* — **so this is the likely
outcome, not the corner case.**

**① C HAS ALMOST NO HEADROOM.** §11A's band table puts **200–300% at 30,120 – 45,179**. Shape C's
line 23 is **45,045.32** — 🔴 **about 134 of household income below the top of the band.** ⚠️ **On a
return where Tier 1 alone is 3,253.20, Tier 2 is 8,048.05 and TWO bank statements are still missing**
*(September and December)*, **that margin is nothing.**

**② 🔴 AND C IS THE ONE SHAPE WHERE §11A'S WARNING ACTUALLY BITES — WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE
EARLIER DRAFT SAID.** Worked through the cascade §11A reproduces *(each step subtracts that band's own
cap from line 14 and re-tests)*:

| Shape | The cascade | Worksheet X line 25 | ATX's Table 5 lookup | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | 40,687.42 − 160.59 → 269% | **975** | 269% → 200–300 row → **975** | ✅ agree |
| **B** | 40,687.42 − 975 → 263% | **975** | 259% → 200–300 row → **975** | ✅ agree |
| 🔴 **C** | 46,670.32 − 975 → **303%, ≥300 so CONTINUE**; − 1,625 → **299%** | 🔴 **1,625** | Form 8962 line 5 = **299%** → 200–300 row → 🔴 **975** | 🔴 **DIVERGE BY 650** |

🛑 **In shape C, Pub. 974's Worksheet X gives 1,625 and ATX's automatic Table 5 lookup gives 975 —
and Pub. 974 says Worksheet X governs** *("Do not enter an amount from Table 5 in the Form 8962
instructions", quoted in §13F)*. 🔑 **ATX would under-state the repayment by 650**, which is exactly
what §11A's sentence below predicts.

🛑 **AND §11A ALREADY NAMED THAT AS THE LIMIT OF ITS OWN CHECK, verbatim:**
> *"At 378% both give 1,625, so ATX's automatic Table 5 lookup lands correctly — ⚠️ **but had the
> Worksheet X percentages fallen below 300, the two would diverge and ATX would be wrong. Verify line
> 28 rather than assume it.**"*
ⓘ *Quoted to the end of §11A's sentence. An earlier draft stopped at "diverge" with no ellipsis —
which this paper criticises §13F's own source for doing.*
✅ **AND §11A ALREADY COMPUTED SHAPE A's POSITION INDEPENDENTLY:** its **"THE BOUND"** paragraph
works an AGI of **40,526 = 269% of FPL** — 🔑 **which is shape A's line 23 to the dollar** — and finds
*"the Table 5 cap at 269% is 975, not 1,625"*. ✅ **So shape A was already modelled once and lands
where the table above puts it**, which is what validates the model. ⛔ **It says NOTHING about shape
C**, whose 299% is the case that diverges. ⚠️ *This sentence read "the position his figures produce…
lands where §16H lands" until 2026-09-22 — written when there was thought to be ONE position.*

🔴 **So the one validation this paper has for trusting the software's automatic lookup no longer
covers the position his figures produce.** ⛔ **The repayment must be worked through Pub. 974
Worksheet X's Part III cascade and compared against what ATX computes**, rather than assumed to
agree. 🔑 **§13F says the same thing in advance** — *"knowing the route still matters for the next
client, whose percentages may straddle a band edge."*

⚠️ **EVERY FIGURE IN THIS SECTION IS CONTINGENT ON THE ATTACHMENTS** *(§16A)* **and on §16E and §16F**,
either of which moves the income. ⛔ **Nothing here is keyed, and none of it is quoted to the client.**
🔑 **§13F's standing rule governs the sequence: THE INCOME AND THE EXPENSES GO ON TOGETHER, IN ONE
PASS.**

### 17C · 🔴 THE 283.80 IS EXPLAINED — and it is a 550.00 exclusion, not a rounding gap

**§16E could only say he reports 283.80 less than the three 1099s. The note decomposes it exactly:**

| | Amount |
|---|---|
| The three Forms 1099-NEC *(§3B, the firm's own copies)* | **19,530.00** |
| 🔴 **LESS: one VoiceCapital inflow of 12 December, excluded as "personal"** | **−550.00** |
| **PLUS: Voxago LLC, which issued no 1099** | **+266.20** |
| **= his line 1** | **19,246.20** ✅ |
| **Net shortfall vs the 1099s** | **283.80** = 550.00 − 266.20 |

🔑 **AND THE BIGGER GAP DECOMPOSES TOO — this is the one the §16E statement actually has to
explain.** §16E measured **3,537.00** against what this paper had established *(the 1099s plus §13E
Tier 1)*. **It is exactly the two exclusions:** `2,987.00` *(Trusted Credit Repair, which §17I① now
establishes is income)* **+** `550.00` *(the VoiceCapital inflow)* **= 3,537.00.** ⛔ **Both are
amounts he received and left off; neither is a rounding difference.**

📖 **The note's own words:** *"Один VOICECAPITAL inflow $550 от 12.12.2025 исключен как personal
согласно листу Zelle Confirmed ('All except one $550 inflow specifically marked personal')."*

🔑 **AND IT RECONCILES AGAINST THIS PAPER PERFECTLY, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT CERTAIN.** §13E Tier 1
counts **VoiceCapital Inc (11)** deposits = **9,084.50**. The note counts **10** = **8,534.50**.
**11 − 1 = 10, and 9,084.50 − 550.00 = 8,534.50.** ⛔ **Same payer, same year, one deposit removed.**
⚠️ **ONE OTHER COUNT DOES *NOT* AGREE, and it is named here because the paragraph above rests on a
count:** §13E records **`Pro Title Agency LLC` (2)** deposits at 700.00; the note records **(1)** at
700.00. **The AMOUNT agrees and the COUNT does not.** ⛔ **Nothing turns on it today** — 700.00 is on
both sides — **but it is unexplained, and §17H is about to ask which activity that 700.00 belongs
to.**

🛑 **WHY THIS IS THE SHARPEST ITEM IN THE WHOLE FILE — stated to §3B's own limit, which an earlier
draft overran.** The VoiceCapital 1099 is not a document the client produced: **the firm PREPARED
it**, through tax1099.com, for VoiceCapital as a payer-client *(§3B, §14A)*. **It reports 9,084.50
in box 1.** ⛔ **So the firm would be signing a return that reports less from that payer than a form
the firm itself produced.**
⚠️ **AND STOP THERE. §3B is explicit and an earlier draft of this section wrote "issued" and "the
IRS holds both" — both of which §3B refuses:** *"What is established is that the FIRM PREPARED it.
⛔ Not 'issued' — issued means furnished to the recipient, and that is not established. Whether it
was transmitted to the IRS and furnished to him is a smaller, separate question; the file is the
software's own output… **not a transmittal receipt**."*
🔑 **THE DISTINCTION IS NOT PEDANTRY — IT DECIDES WHAT KIND OF ITEM THIS IS.** **If the form was
transmitted**, the IRS holds a matching document and this is **AUR exposure**. **If it was not**, it
is an **internal consistency problem** — the firm contradicting its own work product — which still
has to be explained but carries no notice. 🛠️ **ESTABLISHING WHICH IS A FIRM ACTION** *(§17J)*: the
tax1099 account shows transmittal status per form.
⚖️ **It is not for a session to decide whether the 550 is personal** — that is a fact about what the
payment was for, and only he knows. 🔴 **But it cannot simply be netted out in silence**, and it is
**exactly** what the §16E statement has to explain.

**THE TRAIL, end to end:**

| Step | What happened |
|---|---|
| **1 · the client's first document** | car insurance for the **full year = 3,216.51** |

| **3 · his reconciliation note** | that 80% split **70% catering / 30% credit repair** → **1,801.25** and **771.96** |
| **4 · his P&L row label** | a row reading only **`Insurance`**, which is **what Lilian was reading** |
| **5 · Lilian's reading** | it is the **car policy**, so it is a car and truck expense |
| **6 · Lilian's ruling** | ⛔ **NOT Schedule C line 15. It belongs inside the vehicle expenses on LINE 9** |
| **7 · her own annotation** | she renamed the row **`Insurance (vehicle)`** in both of his workbooks, and wrote `Vehicle insurance total 3216.51` beside it |

✅ **AND HER INFERENCE IS CORROBORATED BY THE DOCUMENT, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE IT TURNS A JUDGEMENT
INTO A FACT.** ⛔ **An earlier draft of this section wrote *"he never said it was the vehicle"*. That
is true of the P&L ROW — which is what she had in front of her — and FALSE of the package as a
whole.** 📖 **`PnL-2025-reconciliation.md` carries the insurance inside a table headed
`Автомобиль (Hyundai Tucson): 80% business, далее 70% catering / 30% credit repair`**, row
`Insurance (12 мес) | $3,216.51 | $2,573.21 | $1,801.25 | $771.96`. 🔑 **His own preparer identified
it as the car policy and derived exactly the figures she reached independently**, and this paper had
already treated it as vehicle insurance at **§13G⑤** and **§17E**. ⚖️ **So the ruling stands on the
document, not only on her reading** — which is the stronger place for it to stand.

✅ **THE RULING IS RIGHT, AND THE FORM ITSELF SAYS SO.** Schedule C **line 9 is `Car and truck
expenses`**; under the actual-expense method that line carries fuel, oil, repairs, **insurance**,
registration and tyres. **Line 15 is `Insurance (other than health)`** — liability, property, the
insurance of the business that is not the car. 🔑 **Splitting one vehicle claim across two lines also
hides it from the vehicle questions**, because **Part IV is triggered by line 9**, not by line 15.

🛑 **AND PUTTING IT ON LINE 9 EXPOSES SOMETHING THE RETURN CANNOT KEEP AS IT IS:**

|---|---|---|---|

| **Insurance** | 3,216.51 | **1,801.25 ✅ in** *(on line 15)* | **771.96 🔴 out** |

### 18D · 🔴 FORM 8995 — THE CREDIT-REPAIR BUSINESS IS NOT ON IT

**What the form prints:**

| Line | Prints | Should be |
|---|---|---|
| **1(a) i** | `Sch C: Catering` | ✅ |
| **1(c) i** | **(3,161)** | ✅ |
| 🔴 **1(a)(c) ii** | 🔴 **EMPTY** | 🔴 **the credit-repair business, at ≈ 12,014** |
| 2 | (3,161) | 8,853 |
| 3 | (2,005) | (2,005) |
| 4 | 0 | 6,848 |
| **15 · QBI DEDUCTION** | 🔴 **0** | **1,370** |
| **16 · carryforward to 2026** | 🔴 **(5,166)** | **0** |

🧮 **AND THE (3,161) IS THE PROOF IT IS A LINKING FAULT, NOT A JUDGEMENT.** Catering's Schedule C
line 31 is **(3,401)**, not (3,161). The difference is **240**, and it reconciles exactly:

> `673 × (3,401 ÷ 9,526) = 240.3` — catering's **pro-rata share of the deductible half of SE tax**,
> which reduces QBI. `−3,401 + 240 = −3,161.` ✅

🔑 **The 9,526 in that denominator is the COMBINED profit of both businesses.** ⛔ **So the software
computed catering's QBI knowing the credit-repair business exists — and then printed no row for it.**
ⓘ *That is this session's derivation: the printed package carries **no QBI worksheet**, so the
reconciliation cannot be read off the return. It is offered because it ties to the cent, not because
a form says it.*
✅ **AND THE SAME RULE IS CORROBORATED BY THE PREVIOUS VERSION OF THIS RETURN.** §12B records the
one-Schedule-C Form 8995 printing **18,151** on line 1(c) against a net profit of **19,531** —
🧮 **19,531 − 1,380 = 18,151**, the deductible half of SE tax, taken off in full because there was
only one business. **Same rule, apportioned; and the apportionment is where the second business shows
through.**

🛠️ **AT THE KEYBOARD — where to look, in order.** ⚠️ *This is where the finding stops being
established and becomes a route to test: nothing on a printed return shows an input-screen flag
(§12A's own limit).*
1. **The per-activity QBI setting on Schedule C #2.** In ATX each Schedule C carries its own
   *"qualified business"* election that feeds Form 8995. **Most likely cause** — catering has it, the
   new form does not, and a form added later is exactly where a default gets missed.
2. **Line B, the activity code, is blank on Schedule C #2** *(§18H)*. Fill it in and re-open the
   8995; a form the software treats as incomplete may not link.
3. **Then re-read Form 8995 line 1 and confirm TWO rows print.**

💵 **WHAT IT COSTS, with nothing else changed:** QBI deduction **1,370**, taxable income
**14,529**, tax **1,505**, ⛔ **amount owed 2,494 instead of 2,656 — 162 of tax.**
🔴 **AND THE CARRYFORWARD IS THE WORSE HALF.** A **(5,166)** qualified-business-loss carryforward
into 2026 is not this year's deduction lost — **it silently reduces next year's too.** **Nobody
re-derives a carryforward; it is simply typed in from last year's line 16**, which is what §7 exists
to hold. 🛑 **§7 IS UPDATED IN THIS PASS**: it read *"2025 line 16 = (0) — nothing carries into
2026"*, verified off the one-Schedule-C version on 2026-09-17, **and the split has falsified it.**
⛔ *(2024 handed forward **(2,005)** — that is the figure on line 3 of the current form. A different
year and a different number; an earlier draft of this sentence conflated the two.)*

### 18E · ✅ IS THE CREDIT-REPAIR ACTIVITY QBI? **YES** — and the hard question is moot at his income

**Lilian's question, answered in three steps:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **1 · Is it a trade or business?** | ✅ **Yes** — it is on a Schedule C with **19,531** of receipts and material participation ticked. §13C confirms the activity from three independent places in his own records |
| **2 · Is it a SPECIFIED SERVICE trade or business (SSTB)?** | **Almost certainly not.** §199A(d)(2) lists health · law · accounting · actuarial science · performing arts · **consulting** · athletics · financial services · brokerage · investing and investment management · trading · dealing in securities, **plus** a business whose principal asset is the reputation or skill of its owner — which the regulations confine to endorsements, licensing a likeness, and appearance fees. ⚠️ **Credit repair is on none of them.** The only one worth naming is **consulting**, and only if his work is *advising* clients rather than *executing* disputes |
| **3 · 🔑 AND IT DOES NOT MATTER HERE** | ⛔ **The SSTB limitation does not begin to apply until taxable income exceeds the threshold — 197,300 single for 2025**, the figure printed on Form 8995 itself. **Below it, an SSTB gets the full 20% like any other business.** His taxable income before the QBI deduction is **15,899** |

✅ **SO: credit repair is qualified business income, and it belongs on Form 8995 line 1.**
⛔ **What is NOT settled here is the OTHER business.** **Catering's (3,401) is a qualified business
LOSS**, and §16D/§17E raise a real question whether catering was a trade or business in 2025 at all.
🔑 **If it was not, it leaves the 8995 entirely and the QBI deduction gets larger, not smaller** —
that is §6 ㉞'s shape question, not this one.
⚠️ **BUT NOT LARGER ON THE RETURN AS A WHOLE, and §18I's own headline is why:** removing catering
also adds its loss **back** to AGI, which pushes him **up** the poverty-line scale, where a step in
the repayment cap is worth more than the QBI line. 🔗 **§16H worked exactly that shape and put him at
299 — one point below the boundary.** ⛔ **So "it gets larger" is true of Form 8995 alone and must not
be read as true of the tax.**

### 18F · 🔴 SCHEDULE 1 LINE 17 IS STILL BLANK — and the split is exactly what §16G said would save it

⛔ **NOT A NEW FINDING.** §12A already recorded *"1,625 on Schedule 1 line 17"* among the §11 figures
that never reached the print. 🆕 **What is new is that §16G's prediction can now be TESTED, and it
holds.**

📖 **§16G:** *"§162(l) collapses on a COMBINED Schedule C — and splitting is what saves it"*, because
Pub. 974 Worksheet W line 15 caps the deduction at the **earned income of the business the plan sits
under**. 🔑 **On the return as prepared that business shows 12,927**, so the limit is far above the
figure. ✅ **Shape B is what was keyed, and shape B is the one that preserves the deduction.**

🔴 **BUT §11A's 1,625 MUST BE RE-DERIVED AT THE NEW INCOME, AND IT IS NOT A TRANSCRIPTION.**
§11A computed it when AGI was **58,677 (389% of the poverty line)**. **The deduction here equals the
APTC repayment limitation** — because 1095-A column A equals column C, so the only premium he is
treated as paying is the repayment *(§11A's own caution: "do not carry this to another client")* —
🔑 **and that limitation is a STEP FUNCTION of the poverty-line percentage.**

| Position | % of FPL | Repayment cap, Single | 🔴 **§162(l)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The return as prepared** | **327** | 1,625 | **1,625** |
| 🔴 **With the missing expenses added** | **294** | **975** | 🔴 **975** |

⚠️ **AND IT IS CIRCULAR** — the deduction lowers AGI, which lowers the percentage, which lowers the
cap, which lowers the deduction. ✅ **It converges in one further pass** and the worksheet at §18I
carries the check: **claim 1,625 → 290% → cap 975; claim 975 → 294% → cap 975.** ✅ **975 is the fixed
point.**

🛑 **AND A GATE COMES FIRST, WHICH IS A CLIENT QUESTION NOBODY HAS PUT.** §162(l) is unavailable for
**any month** he was eligible for subsidised coverage through an employer. ⚠️ **He has FOUR W-2
employers** *(§3A)*. **Until he answers, the line stays blank and that is correct.**

### 18G · 🏠 THE HOME OFFICE — the areas are not in his document either, and 124.28 of it IS on the return, in the wrong place

**Lilian's question was whether the apartment area and the office area could be got out of the
document the client sent. 🔴 THE ANSWER IS NO.**
⛔ **AND THIS IS A SECOND ASK, NOT A FIRST ONE.** 📨 **§14H item 2a records both numbers as ASKED on
2026-09-20**, and **§16C item 1 records what came back on 2026-09-22: *"30% allocation"* — the
percentage again.** 🔑 **What is new on 2026-09-23 is only the SEARCH of the three attachments** —
they were the last place the figures could have been hiding, and they are not there. ⚠️ **So the
message to him is a re-ask with the two numbers spelled out, not a fresh question**, and
`method.md`'s *look before you ask* is satisfied by the search below rather than by the asking.

🔎 **SAY WHAT THE SEARCH WAS** *(`method.md` rule 1b)*. **What was searched:** the reconciliation note
`PnL-2025-reconciliation.md` **in full**, and both `DRAFT_2` P&L workbooks **cell by cell**, for
`sq`, `square`, `footage`, `кв`, `метр`, `площад`. 🔴 **Zero hits in all three.** **There is no floor
area, no room dimension and no room count anywhere in them.** ⛔ **This speaks for the three files
Lilian attached on 2026-09-22 and for nothing else** — §14E's earlier record of what he said stands
unchanged, and it is a **percentage** with no measurement behind it.

✏️ **SO SHE HAS TO ASK HIM.** **The two numbers, and they are the whole ask:**
1. **the total living area of the AVA Bonterra apartment, in square feet**;
2. **the area of the room used regularly and exclusively for the business, in square feet.**

⌨️ **WHY A PERCENTAGE CANNOT BE TYPED.** **Form 8829 line 1** is *"Area used regularly and exclusively
for business"* and **line 2** is *"Total area of home"* — **the form computes the percentage from
them**. The **simplified method** asks on Schedule C line 30 for *"the total square footage of (a)
your home"* and *"(b) the part of your home used for business"*. 🔑 **Both routes want square feet.
Neither has a box for "30%."**

🔴 **AND A SECOND THING, WHICH IS NOT ABOUT THE AREAS AT ALL: HIS HOME-OFFICE CLAIM IS SPLIT ACROSS
TWO ROWS, AND ONE OF THEM REACHED THE RETURN.** 📖 **Schedule C Part II's own heading:** *"Expenses.
Enter expenses for business use of your home only on line 30."* ⛔ **Business use of the home may
never appear on lines 8–27** — it goes on **line 30**, through Form 8829 or the simplified method,
and nowhere else.

| His P&L row | Amount | What it is | On the return |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rent/Lease` | **3,655.79** | 30% of Jan–May rent | ✅ **NOT claimed** — line 20b is blank, and so is line 30 |
| 🔴 **`Utilities/Telephone`** | 🔴 **124.28 of the 688.89** | 30% of the Jan–May FPL electricity | 🔴 **CLAIMED, on line 25** |

🛑 **SO THE RETURN DID MAKE THIS MISTAKE, ON THE SMALLER HALF — and an earlier draft of this section
said flatly that it did not.** 📖 **His note routes it there in terms:** *"FPL (электричество)
янв-май … 30% = **$124.28** → строка Utilities/Telephone в credit repair P&L"*, and
**688.89 = 564.61 + 124.28** — 🔗 **arithmetic §17B had already checked and ticked on 2026-09-22.**
⌨️ **THE FIX: Schedule C #2 line 25 = `565`**, the telephone share alone. **The 124.28 joins the
3,655.79 and waits for line 30.**
⚠️ **It is a fix that RAISES his tax**, which is why it is easy to leave — and it is still a defect:
the line-30 route carries a gross-income limit and a form that line 25 does not.
🔑 **AND IT CHANGES THE SIZE OF THE CLAIM: the home office is 3,780.07, not 3,655.79** — so §6 ㊲'s
figure is the rent only, and computing the regular method at 3,780.07 without removing the 124.28
**double-counts it.**

🧮 **WHAT EACH ROUTE WOULD GIVE, once the areas arrive:**

| Route | On his figures |
|---|---|
| **Regular · Form 8829** | 30% of rent 12,185.97 + 30% of FPL electricity 414.27 = **3,780.07** — ⚠️ **only if the 30% is real and the space is exclusive** |
| **Simplified · Schedule C line 30** | **5.00 per square foot, maximum 300 sq ft — a 1,500 ceiling for a full year**, and ⚠️ **prorated on average monthly square footage, so five months of an office is five-twelfths of it** |

🛑 **AND A LIMIT THAT BINDS BOTH: the home-office deduction cannot create or increase a LOSS.** It is
capped at the gross income of that business less its other expenses. ✅ **Credit repair has profit, so
there is room.** ⛔ **Catering has none at all — a second reason it cannot be allocated there**,
independent of §6 ㉞.
⛔ **AND DO NOT ASK HIM THE THREE OBVIOUS FOLLOW-UPS — TWO ARE ALREADY ANSWERED AND THE THIRD IS
OURS.** *(An earlier draft of this section put all three back in the message, which is exactly the
guard `method.md` rule 1 exists for.)* ✅ **Exclusive use** — §14E has his own words, *"one bedroom
used **exclusively** as office"*. ✅ **After May** — he excluded it himself, *"RIVR Lofts / Livble,
Jun–Dec: **0% automatic** · Studio; work area mixed personal use"*. ⚖️ **Which business** — he
assigned 100% to credit repair and left it for us to confirm; **§6 ㉞ holds that as the FIRM's
decision, not a client ask.** 🔑 **So the message carries two numbers and nothing else.**
🔗 **§14E has the rest of the ask, including the month-by-month rent schedule §13G① shows cannot be
derived from the transactions.**

### 18H · ⚠️ THREE SMALLER THINGS ON THE FACE OF THE FORMS

**① 🔴 THE CATERING ACTIVITY CODE IS WRONG, AND IT CAME ACROSS FROM 2024.**
**Line B reads `722410`.** 📖 **Read off the 2025 Instructions for Schedule C, Principal Business
Activity Codes:** `722410` is **`Drinking places (alcoholic beverages)`**. **Caterers is `722300`,
`Special food services (including food service contractors & caterers)`.**
⚠️ **It changes no figure** — the code is a statistical classification — **but it misdescribes the
business on the face of the form**, and §3C already records 722410 as inherited from the 2024 return
rather than chosen.
⛔ **AND THIS IS NOT A NEW FINDING: §6 ㉕ has held it since 2026-09-18**, alongside line A. 🆕 **What
2026-09-23 changes is that the split RESOLVES the line A half** *(each form now describes what it is
for)* **and makes the line B half worse** *(② below)*. ㉕ **is enriched rather than duplicated.**

**② ⛔ SCHEDULE C #2 HAS NO ACTIVITY CODE AT ALL.** ✅ **Line A is filled — it reads `Credit
repair`** *(an earlier reading of this return recorded line A as blank; it is not)*. 🔴 **Line B is
empty.** **The instructions require a six-digit code.** 🛠️ **The candidates, from the `Administrative & Support` and
`Other Professional, Scientific & Technical` blocks of the 2025 list — what was searched, not a
ranking:** `561490 Other business support services` · `541990 All other professional, scientific &
technical services` · ⚠️ **`561450 Credit bureaus` and `561440 Collection agencies`, which sit
immediately beside 561490 and are the two the name invites** — ⛔ **a credit bureau COMPILES credit
histories for creditors and a collection agency PURSUES debts; he does neither**, which is why they
are named and rejected rather than left unmentioned. ⚖️ **None of them changes the tax** — 🔗 **but see §18D, where a blank line B
is one of the two things to test as the cause of the Form 8995 fault.**

| Where | What is there | What it must be |
|---|---|---|
| **Schedule C line 25** | the client's `565 / 689`, with 124.28 of FPL inside the 689 | **565 / 565** — phone only |
| **Form 8829 line 21** | her `565 / 690`, which is **86% TELEPHONE** | ⛔ **no telephone at all** — the FULL Jan–May FPL, **414.27**, and the form applies the percentage |

home utilities, **Jan–May only**.

something any document says** *(`method.md` rule 1b)*: the client's own two utilities figures added
together are `564.61 + 688.89 = 1,253.50`, and `1,253.50 ÷ 1,932.77 = 64.9%`. 🧮 **So 65% is a
RECONCILING percentage — it reproduces his total rather than replacing it** *(1,255.80 against
1,253.50, two dollars apart)*. ⚠️ **Which is exactly why the error survived: a calculation built to
land on his total also lands on what was wrong inside it.**

differ ONLY because the credit-repair one had the electricity folded into it.** 📖 **Strip it and his

business.

`(1,128.89 + 199.60) × 85% = 1,129.22 ÷ 2 = 564.61`.

> — a negative asserted about a document without searching it — and it reached Lilian in the
> workbook before the independent review caught it.** ⓘ *Recorded in full because the paper's value
> depends on its negatives being trustworthy.*

✅ **WHAT THE RETURN ACTUALLY CARRIES: a Form 8829 for EACH business, both on the regular method.**

|---|---|---|
| **1** area used regularly and exclusively | **300** | **300** |

| **8** from Schedule C line 29 | **(1,079)** | **12,338** |
| **15** line 8 less line 14 | **0** *(zero or less)* | **12,338** |

return. 🔑 **Lilian's *"puse área de manera arbitraria para que diera el 30%"* refers to Form 8829
Part I — lines 1 and 2, `300 / 1,000`** — **where the RATIO is exactly what matters.** ⛔ **An earlier
draft's teaching point, that the ratio is irrelevant, is true of the simplified method and inverted
for the method actually in use.**

### 🔴 19D-i · THE REAL DEFECT: THE BUSINESS PERCENTAGE IS APPLIED TWICE, AND THE PHONE IS ON FORM 8829

📖 **Form 8829's column (b) is `Indirect expenses` — expenses of the WHOLE home — and the form
applies the business percentage ITSELF, at line 24.** ⛔ **The figures entered on lines 19 and 21 are
already allocated:**

| Entered | What it already is | Then line 24 multiplies it by |
|---|---|---|
| **Rent 858 / 1,048** | `6,353.38 × 30% = 1,906`, split **45/55** | 🔴 **30% AGAIN** |
| **Utilities 565 / 690** | `1,932 × 65% = 1,255.80`, split **45/55** | 🔴 **30% AGAIN** |

🧮 **So credit repair's home office is roughly `30% × 55% × (30% × rent + 65% × utilities)`** — about
**16.5% of a 30% figure**, which is why 521 came out where the correct answer is several times that.
🔴 **AND WORSE THAN THE DOUBLE MULTIPLICATION: 1,129.22 of the 1,255.80 on line 21 IS THE TELEPHONE**,
which is not a business use of the home at all and may not appear on Form 8829 in any column.
🔗 **§19C.**

⌨️ **WHAT FORM 8829 MUST CARRY.** Column (b) takes the **FULL household expense** and the form does
the rest:

| Line | What to enter | Where it comes from |
