### 19H · 📤 DELIVERED, AND THE CONTROL CAUGHT A DEFECT IN THE MODEL ITSELF

📤 **`Volzhanskiy-2025-worksheet.xlsx` rebuilt and delivered 2026-09-24, then RE-ISSUED the same day
after review** *(the first issue was built on the simplified-method premise §19D now corrects, and
carried the manufactured maintenance gap of §19F)*.
✅ **What it does meet of [`tax-return-sop`](../../../.claude/skills/tax-return-sop/) §4D:** the
principal column is **`What to enter`**, filled on every line row; the **`Action`** vocabulary
*(OK · CHANGE · ADD · CONFIRM · DECIDE · COMPUTED)* is used as defined, with the superseded value
only inside `Action detail`; **no "what it used to say" column**; one frozen header per sheet.
⛔ **What it does NOT yet meet, stated because an earlier draft claimed §4D compliance flatly:** rows
are addressed by **line only**, not `form · page · part · line`; there is **no TYPED / COMPUTED
mark**; and there is **no entry-route sheet** — ⚠️ **which bites hardest on line 30, the one line
that cannot be typed where it appears**, because it is computed from Form 8829's own inputs.
⛔ **Client figures — handed over, never committed.**

✅ **THE CONTROL EARNED ITS KEEP, AND IT FOUND THE MODEL'S OWN ERRORS RATHER THAN THE RETURN'S —
TWICE.**
**First:** with the fix input zeroed the engine reproduced the return on every line **except the SE
tax, by one dollar.**
🔑 **The cause: Schedule SE rounds at EACH line and computes 12.4% and 2.9% SEPARATELY, not 15.3% in
one step.** `10,738 × 0.9235 = 9,916.54 → 9,917`; `× 12.4% = 1,230`; `× 2.9% = 288`; **sum 1,518** —
where a single-step calculation gives **1,517**. ⚠️ **§18I's model carried the single-step form and
happened to agree on that year's figures.**
🔴 **Second, found in review and of exactly the same species: the income-tax line was computed from
the BRACKETS, where the return uses the TAX TABLE's $50-band midpoint** — and §4D records that too.
🧮 **At the return's own taxable income the two agree at 1,613**, which is why the control passed;
**they diverge in every other column** *(1,624 against **1,625**; 1,333 against **1,331**; 846
against **838**)*. ⚠️ **§18I disclosed this as an honest exception; an earlier draft of §19H dropped
the disclosure and claimed the engine reproduced the return on every line.**
✅ **Both corrected. The model now reads the table midpoint, the control still reproduces the return
exactly, and all formulas evaluate with zero errors.**
🔑 **THE PATTERN IS WORTH NAMING, because it has now caught this session three times: a generator
that validates itself against the filed return sails straight through whenever two conventions
happen to coincide at that one point.** ⛔ **A control that passes proves the engine agrees with the
return at ONE income, not that it is right.**
ⓘ *LibreOffice still cannot load a spreadsheet in this container — not even a two-column CSV — so the
workbook carries no cached values and is flagged `fullCalcOnLoad`; Excel computes it on open, and the
verification above was run independently in Python.*

## 20 · 💳 THE CARD INTEREST — and the email that went to the client — 2026-09-24 (later)

businesses, because the client asked her for help with it.** ⚠️ **The split turned out to be the
easy half.** 🛑 **The hard half is HOW MUCH is deductible at all — and that is where the money is.**
🔗 **Delivered as tab `10. Card interest` of the worksheet.**

### 20A · 📧 THE EMAIL IS ON THE RECORD — and three things it does not ask

`lilian@` to the client, CC `julia@`.** ✅ **Found in Julia's mailbox exactly as she said.** It asks:

| **5** | **The apartment area and the office area in square feet**, saying explicitly that the 30% does not answer it | **㊳** — ✅ **and it is asked BETTER this time: it names both numbers separately and says why the percentage is not enough** *(§16C item 1 recorded that the first ask did not land)* |

  matters now.** §19D's Form 8829 rebuild needs the **actual Jan–May rent**, and §15D found direct

add.**

### 20B · 🚗 THE AUTO-LOAN INTEREST IS A SCHEDULE C LINE 16b QUESTION — ⛔ NOT Schedule 1-A

⚠️ **This needs settling BEFORE the figure arrives, because 2025 is the first year the wrong answer
is even available.** **Schedule 1-A Part IV, "No Tax on Car Loan Interest", is brand new for 2025**,
and a preparer who sees "car loan interest" on a new line will put it there.

🛑 **HE DOES NOT QUALIFY, and it is not close.** The 2025 Instructions for Form 1040, Schedule 1-A
Part IV, list five requirements **word for word**:

> **1.** *Your loan was originated after December 31, 2024.*
> **2.** *The loan was originated by you.*
> **3.** *The proceeds from your loan were used to purchase an APV (lease payments do not qualify).*
> **4.** *Your APV is for personal use (which means you don't expect it to be used predominantly for
> business or commercial use...).*
> **5.** *Your loan is secured by a first lien on the purchased APV.*

**TWO of the five fail on facts already in the file** — and the third row below is **not a sixth
requirement** but the separate *Applicable passenger vehicle* definition, which fails either way:

| | Requirement | The file says |
|---|---|---|
| **①** | **Loan originated after 31 Dec 2024** | 🔴 **The car is a 2021 Hyundai Tucson and the loan is ALREADY PAID OFF** *(§16B item 9)*. ⛔ **A loan originated in 2025 and retired in 2025 on a four-year-old car is not what happened** |
| **②** | **APV is for PERSONAL use — "not predominantly business"** | 🔴 **The return claims 80% BUSINESS.** ⛔ **The two claims are mutually exclusive: one of them has to be abandoned** |
| **③** | *(NOT a requirement — the **APV definition**)* **"The original use of the vehicle starts with you (a used vehicle does not qualify)"**, and **"final assembly in the United States"** | ⚖️ **A DILEMMA, NOT AN UNKNOWN — and it closes either way.** If he bought the 2021 Tucson **new**, the loan predates 2025 and requirement ① fails; if he bought it **used**, the APV definition fails. ⓘ *The **VIN** answers the final-assembly test — the instructions say the plant of manufacture is readable from it — but NOT the original-use test, which the purchase documents answer. The deduction also REQUIRES the VIN on the return* |

✅ **WHERE IT DOES GO: Schedule C, at the business-use percentage**, split 70/30 with the rest of
the vehicle pool. ⓘ *Pub. 463 names the **schedule**; the **line — 16b, "other" business interest** —
comes from the Schedule C instructions' own "How to report".* **Pub. 463, word for word:**

> *"You may be able to take a deduction for interest paid on a car loan **even if you use the
> standard mileage rate**... if you are self-employed and use your car in your business, you can
> deduct that part of the interest expense that represents your business use of the car. For
> example, if you use your car 60% for business, you can deduct 60% of the interest on Schedule C
> (Form 1040)."*

🔑 **So WHERE it goes does not depend on the mileage-vs-actual decision** — line 16b survives either
method, which is why that much can be settled now. ⚠️ **The AMOUNT still waits on ㊴**: it is the
business-use percentage × the interest, and §16C item 2 records that the percentage is **business
miles ÷ total miles**, not the asserted 80%. ⛔ **And §19F holds the whole vehicle pool back for
exactly that reason — this figure travels with it.** ⚠️ **The personal share is NOT deductible anywhere**: §163(h)
personal interest has no Schedule A home either. ⓘ *And where both routes were genuinely available
the instructions allow a choice but forbid taking the same amount twice — that is not this case.*

### 20C · 💳 THE CREDIT-CARD INTEREST — the gate, settled by the year's own arithmetic

**4,338.00 across nine cards, about 360 a month.** 🔗 **§13G⑦ already put the defensible figure at
ZERO**; **§20C is the evidence behind that sentence**, which it did not have.

**The rule, from the 2025 Instructions for Schedule C, Lines 16a and 16b, word for word:**

> *"'Interest allocation' rules require you to allocate (classify) your interest expense so it is
> deducted (or capitalized) on the correct line of your return... Generally, you allocate interest
> expense by **tracing how the proceeds of the loan were used**."*

🛑 **THERE IS NO PERCENTAGE TO APPLY TO A TOTAL.** The rule asks what the borrowed money **bought**.

#### ① The test the file can actually run — ⚠️ and it runs on WINDOWS, not on the year

🛑 **SCOPE FIRST, BECAUSE THE FINDING BELOW IS WORTH NOTHING WITHOUT IT.** 🔗 **§17I① already
established that only ONE of these accounts spans 2025** — four stop in April and four begin between
9 April and 8 May. **So every figure in this table is what the HELD STATEMENTS show for that card's
own window, never "the year".** ✅ **What makes the comparison sound anyway: on each card the
INTEREST rows occupy the SAME window as the CHARGE rows** *(…2861 — four interest postings, 1 Jan to
1 Apr; …2898 — eight, 11 May to 11 Dec)*, **so interest and charges are being read off the same
statements.**

| Card | what the package holds | interest in that window | charges in that window | business-classified | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Capital One Venture …2898 | 9 Apr – 11 Dec | 1,162.72 | 949.98 | 876.85 | 🔴 interest **exceeds** every charge in the window |
| Chase Prime Visa …9590 | 25 Apr – 1 Dec | 826.06 | 473.97 | 419.68 | 🔴 same |
| Capital One Venture …6004 | 5 Jan – 10 Apr | 559.26 | 471.74 | 451.97 | 🔴 same |
| **Apple Card** | **31 Jan – 31 Dec — the ONLY one spanning the year** | 521.75 | 548.73 | 523.77 | |
| Capital One Quicksilver …4894 | 26 Apr – 26 Dec | 493.30 | 305.79 | 247.29 | 🔴 same |
| **Chase Prime Visa …2861** | **1 Jan – 1 Apr** | **413.11** | **0.00** | **0.00** | 🛑 **FOUR INTEREST POSTINGS AND NOT ONE PURCHASE IN ITS WINDOW** |
| Capital One Quicksilver …3996 | 20 Jan – 25 Apr | 242.12 | 240.85 | 240.85 | 🔴 same, by 1.27 |
| Capital One Quicksilver …4886 | 8 May – 14 Dec | 81.38 | 242.27 | 145.39 | |
| Capital One Quicksilver …8888 | 8 Jan – 13 Apr | 38.30 | 99.20 | 69.30 | |
| **TOTAL** | | **4,338.00** | **3,332.53** | **2,975.10** | |

🛑 **ON SIX OF THE NINE CARDS, THE INTEREST IN THE WINDOW THE PACKAGE COVERS EXCEEDS EVERY CHARGE IN
THAT SAME WINDOW** — and on `…2861` there are four interest postings and **no purchase at all** in
its three months. ⚖️ **That is the answer, and the scoping does not weaken it:** interest that
outruns the charges sitting beside it is interest on a balance that **arrived before the window
opened**. ⛔ **So the charges the file holds cannot describe what the debt bought** — and describing
what the debt bought is exactly what the rule requires. ⛔ **A percentage taken off those charges
would be an invention dressed as a method.**

⚠️ **AND `4,338.00` IS A FLOOR, NOT THE YEAR'S TOTAL.** It is the interest **on the statements the
firm holds**. ⓘ *§17I① reads the April cluster as the shape of **card replacement**, which would
make the portfolio's coverage close to complete — but it flags that as a reading of dates, not an
established fact, and it is not relied on here.* 🔑 **A floor is enough for this conclusion and NOT
enough for the opposite one**, which is the asymmetry worth keeping: more interest cannot make
untraceable interest traceable. ⚠️ **It does mean §20D's `1,003` is itself a floor.**

#### ② And of the business charges that ARE there, almost none is established

| | |
|---|---|
| Business-classified by the package *(`Expense` + `Review`; the `Personal` rows are already out)* | **2,975.10** |
| the package classified as `Expense` rather than `Review` | **223.54** |
| ✅ **of which backed by a USER-CONFIRMED RULE** | 🔑 **117.54** |
| 🔴 everything else — **not established as business** | **2,857.56** |

🛑 **THE 223.54 IS NOT A CLIENT CONFIRMATION, AND THE PACKAGE SAYS SO ON ITS FACE.** **All six
`Expense` rows carry `Deduction Rule = "Candidate 100%"` and a note asking for confirmation.**
**The split is:**

  actual entry on the package's `Confirmed Rules` sheet:* **"Microsoft / Adobe / Canva / Google One /
  Dropbox — User confirmed business use."** 🔑 **This is the only figure in the whole exercise
  resting on something the client said.**
- 🔴 **106.00 — `BROWARD CLERKS OFFICE`.** ⛔ **`Confidence = Medium`, and the package's own note is

### 20D · 💰 WHAT IT IS WORTH — ⚠️ and the correction to "no cambia el impuesto"

🔑 **Her premise was *"dado que eso no va a cambiar la cantidad de impuestos que se deben"*.**
✅ **That is TRUE of the SPLIT** — the two Schedule C nets add to the same figure, so the SE tax, the
QBI deduction, AGI and Form 8962 are identical whichever way it is divided. 🔑 **Which is precisely
why a sensible split is enough and a perfect one is not needed.**
🛑 **It is NOT true of the AMOUNT.** Priced on the worksheet's own engine *(tab 8 column D as the
baseline)*:

| Position | Amount owed | Worth |
|---|---|---|
| Line 25 fixed, **no card interest claimed** | **2,803** | — |
| **+ the full 4,338 claimed** | **1,800** | 🔴 **1,003** |
| + a **token 224** — an illustration of the bracket only | **2,753** | **50** |

⛔ **THE THIRD ROW IS NOT A TRACED FIGURE AND MUST NOT BE READ AS ONE.** **224 is a CHARGES total,
not an interest amount**, and deducting it as interest is precisely the "invention dressed as a
method" §20C rejects. 🔑 **There is no traced figure to put there — that is the finding, not a gap
in the table.** ⓘ *The row exists to show how flat the bracket is: a small claim buys almost nothing,
so "claim a little to be safe" is the worst of both positions.*

⚠️ **SO THE SPLIT SHE WAS ASKED ABOUT IS WORTH NOTHING AT ALL — ✅ exactly as she said — and the
AMOUNT underneath it is worth up to about 1,003.** ⓘ *Claiming the full 4,338 takes him to **309%** of the poverty line — closer
to the 300% cliff, but **not across it**, so the 1,625 repayment cap is unchanged. ⛔ **The vehicle
is still the only item that crosses that band** (§19G).*

⚖️ **THE RECOMMENDATION IS ZERO, AND IT IS A RECOMMENDATION, NOT A RULING** — ⚖️ **a position is
Lilian's and Julia's to take** *(delivery rule 7)*. **The reason is not tidiness:** the file cannot
support anything else, and 4,338 on line 16b invites exactly the question nobody in this file can
answer. 🔑 **The client asked for help with this; the honest answer IS the help.**
ⓘ *The alternative is real but expensive: ask him for the card statements showing opening balances
and charge history, and trace. On 223.54 of confirmed business charging spread over nine cards, that
will not repay the hours — and §17I① records that EIGHT of these nine accounts do not span the year.*

expenses** would give roughly **13% / 87%** *(catering 1,079 against credit repair 7,193)* — a very
different answer with no support in the rule. **The interest belongs to the CHARGES the money made,
not to how big each business happens to be.** ⚠️ **Worth writing down because it is the split a
reviewer would reach for first.**

version of tab 10 put the business-classified charges at **1,988.03**. **That was the sum of seven
named tax categories, not the business-classified total**, which is **2,975.10**: `Bank / card fees`
431.00 and `Other expense - needs review` 556.07 had been left out without saying so.

the same turn.** 🔑 **The control that caught it: summing the allocation table's own amount column
and requiring it to tie to the section above it — the corrected tab now does that on the face of the
sheet.** ✅ **The split and the pricing were unaffected**, because both missing pools land on neither
Schedule C. ⓘ *And the correction IMPROVED the case for zero: it is what surfaced the 431.00 of
`PAST DUE FEE` / `FEES CHARGED`.*

🛑 **AND THE INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THIS SECTION FOUND FOUR MORE, ALL CONFIRMED AGAINST THE SOURCES
AND ALL FIXED ABOVE. THREE OF THEM ARE THE SAME FAILURE THIS PAPER KEEPS RECORDING.**

| | What §20's first draft said | What the source says |
|---|---|---|
| **①** | 🔴 *"the 2025 interest is larger than everything charged to that card **ALL YEAR**"*, and one card had *"**no charges whatsoever**"* | ⛔ **AN UNSCOPED NEGATIVE — `method.md` rule 1b, for the SECOND time on this client in two sessions** *(§19I was the first)*. **Eight of the nine accounts do not span 2025**, and `…2861` holds **three months**. 🔗 **§17I① had the coverage table, and §19D-iii says in terms *"SAY WHAT THE SEARCH WAS, NOT WHAT THE WORLD IS"*.** ✅ **The finding survives window-matched** — which is how §20C① now reads |
| **②** | 🔴 *"NOT previously on any list, and it is a good question"* — of the email's second-vehicle question | ⛔ **It is `method.md` rule 1: asking for what he already sent.** **§14D and §14H item 15 both had it**, and the client's `Confirmed Rules` sheet answers it — *"Only vehicle in 2025"*. 🛑 **AND THE ANSWER RUNS AGAINST THE 80%, so the first draft praised a question whose answer damages the position it thought it supported.** ⓘ *It also cited **line 45**; it is **line 46*** |
| **③** | 🔴 *"of which the CLIENT has confirmed as business — 223.54"* | ⛔ **The package denies it on its face:** all six `Expense` rows read `Candidate 100%` with a *"confirm"* note, and the 106.00 has **no `Confirmed Rules` entry** and `Confidence = Medium`. **Client-backed is 117.54.** 🔑 **And §20E already put that same 106.00 on neither Schedule C — the section contradicted itself** |
| **④** | 🔴 *"Three of them fail on facts already in the file"* | ⛔ **Its own table showed two fails and one unknown**, §6 ⑳ said two, and the commit message said two. ✅ **And the "unknown" was answerable as a dilemma** — new or used, it closes either way |

🔑 **THE PATTERN WORTH KEEPING, BECAUSE IT IS NOW THREE FOR THREE: every one of these defects was a
claim about the WORLD where the file only supported a claim about the SEARCH** — *no Form 8829 in the
package* (§19I), *charged all year*, *nobody has asked this*, *the client confirmed it*.
⚠️ **The arithmetic and the quotations were clean in every case; the framing was not.**
⛔ **A section that computes correctly can still be wrong in the only way that reaches a client.**

## 21 · 🔄 THE POSTURE CHANGED — "no auditamos sus clasificaciones" — and it moved three numbers — 2026-09-24 (later still)

🗣️ **Lilian's instruction, and it governs everything below:**
> *"Nuestro trabajo no es auditar los números del cliente. Él no es nuestro cliente de bookkeeping.
> Simplemente tenemos que tomar lo que él nos da y ayudarlo a poner esos números en orden, pero no
> tenemos que revisar sus clasificaciones. No es nuestro trabajo ese. Tenemos que confiar, en cierta
> medida, en lo que cabe en la información que él nos da."*

⚖️ **And on the card interest specifically:** *"necesito que tomemos alguna decisión… que no sea 0%
de deducción… algo con algún fundamento lógico, presentarle eso y luego que ella decida."*
✅ **§20D put the zero as a recommendation and said the position was hers and Julia's to take. She
has taken it.** 🔑 **This section is the non-zero answer, built the way she asked: from his numbers.**

⛔ **WHAT THE POSTURE DOES *NOT* RETIRE, and the distinction is the whole of §21C.** Not auditing his
**classifications** is not the same as ignoring his own **stated totals**. 🔑 **Where his transaction
file's automatic sorting CONTRADICTS a figure he wrote down himself, the figure he wrote down wins** —
that is not second-guessing him, it is reading him.

### 21A · ✅ HER RETURN WAS RE-KEYED, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS CLEAN

**She removed both Schedule C line 25 utilities figures (565 and 689) and rebuilt the home office on
two Forms 8829.** Business income **10,738 → 11,146**; amount owed **2,774 → 2,866**.

🔑 **A model built from her two Schedule C nets ALONE reproduces the whole return line for line** —
SE tax 1,574 · AGI 50,885 · Form 8962 line 5 = 337% · applicable figure **0.0693** · contribution
3,526 · credit 2,339 · excess 3,527 · cap 1,625 · QBI 1,671 · taxable income 15,734 · income tax
1,649 · total tax 4,848 · **owed 2,866**. ✅ **Nothing she keyed is wrong.**

🛑 **AND THE ONE DISAGREEMENT WAS THE CONTROL'S FAULT, NOT HERS.** The model first computed the
applicable figure as **0.0692** against ATX's **0.0693**. **ATX is right.** `0.06 + 37 × 0.00025` is
**0.06925 exactly**, which rounds up; evaluated in binary floating point it is stored as
`0.06924999999999999` and rounds **down**. ⓘ *Same family as the Python `round()` banker's-rounding
defect recorded on the Melnyk return.* ✅ **The workbook formula is now built in integer space** —
`ROUND((240+(fpl−300))*2.5,0)/10000`, where `692.5` is exact in binary — and reproduces **0.0688 at
335%, 0.0690 at 336%, 0.0693 at 337%**, all three matching filed returns.
🔑 **THE LESSON, AND IT IS NEW: a control that disagrees with the return is not evidence against the
return until you know which side is wrong.** §19/§20 recorded controls that passed while wrong; this
is the mirror case — a control that **failed** while the return was right.

portion only — All cards mixed-use; accountant allocates interest to business-financed purchases."*

### 21C · 🔴 THE RENT ON FORM 8829 IS HALF OF WHAT HE PAID — and his own note says so in one line

🛑 **THE LARGEST FINDING OF THE SESSION, and it is a mis-sort inside his file, not a missing document.**

📖 **His reconciliation note, word for word:**
> *"Аренда AVA (фактические платежи из выписок): BILT янв $2,853.07 + фев $2,843.13 (5 платежей) +
> мар $500.00 + AVA BONTERRA PYMT мар $3,119.54 + апр $2,870.23 = **$12,185.97**. 30% = **$3,655.79**"*

**Every one of those nine payments is in his transaction file. Where his file put them:**

| | payments | amount | his file's category |
|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ | seven `BILT PAYMENT BILTRENT` rows, 10 Jan – 6 Mar | **6,196.20** | `Rent / possible home office` |
| 🔴 | `AVA BONTERRA PYMT` **20 Mar** and **17 Apr** | **5,989.77** | ⛔ **`Other expense - needs review`** |
| | | **12,185.97** | ✅ **his own total, to the cent** |

🛑 **So Form 8829 line 19 carries 6,353.38 where it should carry 12,185.97.** The form was built from
his file's automatic sorting, and that sorting filed the two largest rent payments of the year as
"other". 🔑 **HIS OWN P&L already used 12,185.97 (Rent/Lease 3,655.79 = 30%)** — the figure was lost in the
rebuild from the package, not in anyone's arithmetic. ⓘ *And that sheet is itself inconsistent: its
helper columns beside the same row read `Total for home 6,353.38 | 1,906.014`, so it used the note's
total in the P&L line and the package's pool in its own working. §21C relies on the NOTE, which is
the client's own hand, not on either column.*

🛑 **AND THE CLIENT'S OWN TWO CAVEATS ON THIS EXACT LINE TRAVEL WITH THE FIGURE — the posture
requires it.** Four lines below the total he wrote, his note says:

### 21D · 🏠 ONE FORM 8829, NOT TWO — the IRS instruction, read off the current-year source

📖 **2025 Instructions for Form 8829, Line 36, word for word:**
> *"If your home was used in more than one business, allocate the amount shown on line 36 to each
> business using any method that is reasonable under the circumstances. For each business, enter on
> Schedule C, line 30, only the amount allocated to that business."*

🔑 **The sequence is ONE form, computed on the whole home, and THEN line 36 allocated.** Not two forms
with the expenses pre-split 45/55.

🛑 **What the two-form version costs right now:** catering's Form 8829 line 8 is a **loss**, so its
line 27 is **0** and its whole **1,118** is disallowed and carried to 2026 on line 43. **Half the home
office buys nothing this year.** ⚠️ **And each form's Part I claims 300 of 1,000 sq ft, so the two
together assert 600 sq ft of a 1,000 sq ft home.**

✅ **Allocating 100% to credit repair is a "reasonable method" here, and it is the CLIENT'S OWN
PROPOSAL** — ⚠️ *but §18 records it with its rider, which belongs here too: **"his decision, explicitly
left for us to confirm"**, and §6 ㉞ holds it as one of four allocations with **no stated basis**.*
🔑 **The support for it is not his say-so but the facts: catering had NO revenue at all in 2025, so it
is hard to argue the office was used in it, and his own note gives the reason ("desk-based
business").**

⚠️ **AND ONE INCONSISTENCY ON THIS RETURN SHOULD GO TO JULIA WITH IT:** the same return assigns
**70% of the business DRIVING to catering** on his mileage split, while the home office goes **100%
to credit repair** because catering did not trade. **Both positions are his; they sit oddly together
and she should see them side by side.**

> ✏️ **Form 8829 (credit repair): line 19 = 12,186 · line 21 = 414 · line 23 = 12,600 · line 24 =
> line 36 = 3,780.** ⛔ **Delete the catering Form 8829.**

### 21E · 📞 THE PHONE IS ON THE WRONG FORM, AT THE WRONG PERCENTAGE

🔴 **1,128.89 of Verizon and AT&T sits inside Form 8829 line 21**, where the form applies the
**home-office 30%** to it instead of the **85%** the client stated. ⛔ **Wrong twice over.**

📖 **Pub. 587, "Telephone":** the basic local service charge for the first line into the home is a
nondeductible personal expense; business long-distance and a second line used exclusively for
business are deductible — *"**Do not include these expenses as a cost of using your home for
business.** Deduct these charges separately on the appropriate form or schedule."*

⚠️ **AND THE QUOTED PASSAGE CARVES OUT ONE CASE THIS SECTION DOES NOT TEST.** Pub. 587 says the
basic charge for the first telephone **LANDLINE** into the home is nondeductible *at any percentage*.
🔑 **The pool is Verizon Wireless 470.91 + AT&T 657.98.** ✅ *Cell service is outside that rule, and
AT&T at about 81.66 a month reads as fibre internet rather than a landline* — ⛔ **but neither has
been established.** ➡️ **Identify the AT&T account before keying: if it is the residence's first
landline, its basic service charge comes out.**

> ✏️ **Schedule C #2 line 25 Utilities = 960** *(1,128.89 × 85% = 959.56)*.
> ✏️ **And Form 8829 line 21 becomes 414** — the FPL electricity for **January–May only**
> *(97.10 + 88.95 + 78.35 + 77.87 + 72.00 = 414.27)*. ⛔ *The remaining 389.61 of FPL is the RIVR
> period, which he does not claim.*

## 22 · 🏠 "¿DE DÓNDE SACAS ESOS $12,000?" — the question was right, and the answer is a LABEL — 2026-09-24

🗣️ **Lilian, on §21C:** *"El documento de cliente dice que fueron $6,000… ¿Qué te parece si tal vez
simplemente le decimos al cliente que confirme que el costo total de la renta del año fue $6,000?"*

✅ **The instinct — ask him — is right and §22C writes the question out.** ⛔ **The figure to put to
him is NOT 6,000**, and this section exists because §21C asserted 12,185.97 without ever explaining
**why his own file says 6,353.38**. 🔑 **A finding nobody can follow is a finding nobody should act
on.**

### 22A · 🔍 THE ANSWER: his file categorises by the BANK DESCRIPTION, and rent paid by card does not say "rent"

**His package sorts every row into a tax category by reading the description string.**

| how he paid | what the bank prints | what his file called it |
|---|---|---|
| through the **BILT** rent app | `BILT PAYMENT BILTRENT` | ✅ `Rent / possible home office` |
| by **card, straight to the landlord** | `AVA BONTERRA PYMT 877282` | ⛔ `Other expense - needs review` |

🛑 **THE TWO LARGEST RENT PAYMENTS OF THE YEAR WENT BY CARD** — **3,119.54 on 20 March** and
**2,870.23 on 17 April**. 🔑 **They are the two biggest rows in the entire 45,305.99 `Other expense`
bucket**, sitting at the top of it.

**So the whole difference is:**

| | |
|---|---|
| his file's `Rent / possible home office` pool | **6,353.38** |
| ⛔ *of which* **Jun–Dec** charges from after he moved to RIVR — *eight `ERenterplan` insurance debits (151.28) and two `RP Resident` platform fees (5.90)* | −157.18 |
| = the **BILT** payments, Jan–Mar | **6,196.20** |
| 🔴 **plus the two AVA card payments his file filed as "other"** | **+5,989.77** |
| = **what he actually paid the landlord, Jan–Apr** | ✅ **12,185.97** |

✅ **Which is his own note's total, to the cent, and the figure his own P&L used at 30% (3,655.79).**

### 22B · 🧮 AND THE ARITHMETIC PROVES IT IS A LABEL PROBLEM, NOT A FACT

📖 **His `Confirmed Rules` sheet:** *"AVA Bonterra Jan–May 2025 — Home office allocation — 30% — 2BR;
one bedroom used exclusively as office; **rent approx. $2,700/mo**."*

| month | paid to the landlord | |
|---|---|---|
| January | **2,853.07** | one month, through BILT |
| February | **2,843.13** | one month, in five part-payments |
| March | **3,619.54** | 🔴 `500.00` BILT **+** `3,119.54` AVA card |
| April | **2,870.23** | one month, AVA card |
| **May** | ⛔ **nothing** | |
| | **12,185.97** | |

🔑 **January, February and April are each ~2,855 — consistent with his stated ~2,700 plus fees.**
⛔ **And the labelled pool cannot be the rent on its own terms:** the BILT rows run **January to
6 March** and come to **6,196.20** — **2,065 a month across those three months**, against three
complete months that are each ~2,855. 🔑 **The month table above is the real evidence and it needs no
rhetoric: three complete months at ~2,855 and one at 3,619.54 do not add up to 6,353.38 under any
reading.**

that goes on Form 8829 line 21 **includes 72.00 dated 19 May**. ✅ **He was still metered in that
